:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from              [2026-08-17 Mon 09:04] \\
  silent loss of human judgement
CLOCK: [2026-08-17 Mon 09:09]--[2026-08-17 Mon 09:11] =>  0:02
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-17 Mon 09:09] \\
  drop in place instead of refreshing
- [2026-08-17 Mon 09:09]--[2026-08-17 Mon 09:11] stop dismiss discarding assignments
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-17 Mon 09:11] \\
  assignments survive a dismissal
:END:

Reported by the user 2026-08-17: dismissing an item destroys every
assignment made in the buffer during that review session, before
anything has been applied.

*Confirmed by reading, not inferred.*
=claude-code-ide-org-review-dismiss= ends with
=(claude-code-ide-org-review-refresh)=, and refresh's own docstring is
"Rebuild the review buffer from the queue, discarding marks." It
rebuilds =claude-code-ide-org--review-items= from the queue files, so
*everything held only in memory is lost*: assigned headings
(=:id=/=:assigned=), edited intervals (=:start=/=:end=), notes, and
=:stale-confirmed=. The docstring admits to marks alone, which are
selection state and cheap to redo; the rest is *judgement* and is not.

*Why this is the serious one.* The queue's whole premise is that raw
events are durable and re-clusterable while human judgement is the
scarce input -- the asymmetry
[[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] states as "the
queue keeps every raw event and can be re-clustered at will, while
annotations written into =:LOGBOOK:= cannot be re-derived." An
assignment is precisely that scarce judgement, and =d= discards an
arbitrary amount of it while appearing to act on *one line*.

*Orthogonal in mechanism to
[[id:9e80a32d-43e1-4647-a42c-cba10f6e79cc][9e80a32d]], related in kind,
and far more severe.* That one is =--review-render= resetting point;
this one is =--review-refresh= rebuilding the item list. Different
functions, different triggers. What they share is that the review buffer
carries unsaved session state which redrawing and rebuilding do not
preserve, and that the commands differ in whether they bother to. Losing
point costs a keystroke; losing assignments costs the judgement again,
silently, with no undo and no warning.

*Fix direction, unresolved.* =d= has already written the dismissal to
the queue by the time it refreshes, so it does not need a rebuild at
all -- dropping the item from =--review-items= in place and re-rendering
would preserve the session. That also lets it restore point and advance
like =a=, which is where this and 9e80a32d converge in the fix even
though they diverge in the cause. Worth checking whether =x= (apply) has
the same shape for the *unapplied* items it leaves behind.

*Not attempted:* whether =g= should keep assignments too. It is
explicitly the "rebuild from the queue" command and a human types it
deliberately, so discarding there is arguably correct -- but it should
say *assignments*, not "marks", because the current wording undersells
what it throws away.

*Fixed 2026-08-17.* =d= no longer calls =review-refresh=. It drops the
item from =claude-code-ide-org--review-items= in place and re-renders
through the shared path
([[id:2d032000-bf04-47f5-b5e6-ef4299078d15][2d032000]]), landing point on
the item that took its place. The dismissal is already durable in the
queue by then, so the rebuild was adding nothing except the loss.

*The test asserts the surviving item by identity, not by content,* which
is the distinction that matters: a rebuild produces a fresh plist from
the same queue events -- equal in every visible field, and empty of the
decision. Confirmed failing before the fix and passing after (285 tests,
1 unexpected → 0).

*=review-apply= has the same shape and was deliberately left alone.* On
success it refreshes, so decisions on items it did *not* apply are
discarded too. The difference is that apply changes org files: re-reading
the queue afterwards is how the view learns what was actually consumed,
and dropping items in place risks a buffer that disagrees with the file
it just wrote. Dismiss had no such justification. Whether apply can
preserve the untouched items -- probably by removing only what applied
and rebuilding the rest -- is a separate question and is not answered
here.

*Still true, and the reason this mattered more than the point bug:* the
queue's premise is that raw events are cheap and re-derivable while human
judgement is not. Every command that rebuilds from the queue is trading
the expensive thing for the cheap one, and only =g= should be allowed to
make that trade.


* DONE Route review-buffer commands through one mutate-render-restore path :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-17 Mon 09:11]
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       2d032000-bf04-47f5-b5e6-ef4299078d15
:CREATED:  [2026-08-17 Mon 09:07]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-08-17 Mon 17:13
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Clock lifecycle & visibility
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DOING"      from              [2026-08-17 Mon 09:07] \\
  one path for mutate-render-restore
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-17 Mon 09:11] \\
  one redraw path, advance rule recorded
:END:

*Done 2026-08-17.* =claude-code-ide-org--review-redraw= is the single
path: re-render, put point back on the item by identity, and advance when
asked. Every command that mutates an item now goes through it -- =m=/=u=
(advance), =a= (advance), =d= (advance onto the item that replaced it),
=e= and =N= (stay).

*The advance rule, written down so it stops being decided per command:*
advance when the command *answers* the question the line posed, stay when
it does not. Marking, assigning and dismissing decide an item; narrowing
an interval or editing a note are steps taken while still deciding.

*Restores by identity, not line number.* =--review-goto-line= remains for
=--review-set-all=, which acts on every item and so has no single one to
return to. Everything else uses =--review-goto-item=, because assigning
removes the evidence lines an unassigned span carries and the buffer is a
different height afterwards.

*Left calling =--review-render= directly, each deliberately:*
=--review-set-all= (no single item), =review-refresh= (rebuilding is its
purpose), =review-apply= (see below), and =review-dismiss= for its
one branch where nothing remains to land on.

*=review-refresh='s docstring was rewritten* rather than left saying
"discarding marks". It discards every unapplied decision -- assignments,
intervals, notes, stale confirmations. Marks are selection and cheap to
redo; the rest is judgement. =g= is typed deliberately so discarding
there is correct, but it has to say what it costs.

*Boundary held on purpose: =review-apply= was not changed.* It has the
same shape as the dismiss defect -- on success it calls
=review-refresh=, discarding decisions on the items it did *not* apply --
but the justification differs. Apply changes org files, so re-reading the
queue afterwards reflects what was actually consumed, and dropping items
in place would risk a view that disagrees with the file. Dismiss had no
such excuse: it had already written its dismissal to the queue. Recorded
on [[id:73cd5a1b-9b4f-409d-9aab-ffc6ea736ae5][73cd5a1b]] rather than
fixed blind.

  assign advances, interval edit stays
:END:

*Verified, not just implemented* (written 2026-08-17, before archiving —
this heading reached =DONE= with an empty body).

=--review-render= ends with =(goto-char (point-min))=, so every command
that re-renders has to put point back. The mark commands already did, and
their ADVANCE flag exists because its absence was "the single biggest
complaint after the first real by-hand apply". Assign and interval-edit
never got the same treatment.

*Shipped in =0042615=.* Assign now advances — assigning *answers* the
question the line posed — while interval-edit stays on the item, since
=e= is the step taken before assigning rather than after. Point is
restored *by identity, not line number*: assigning removes the evidence
lines an unassigned span carries, so the buffer is shorter afterwards and
the old line number points at something else. =--review-goto-item= is
that lookup.

*A second defect, found by the new test rather than by reading.* Building
the candidate list parsed =(or (org-entry-get nil "CREATED") "")=, and
parsing the empty string *signals* — inside a block wrapped in
=ignore-errors=. So a heading with no =:CREATED:= was dropped from the
list entirely, despite the comment beside it claiming such headings are
kept. Invisible in production, where every heading has one; only a
fixture without one exposed it. Measured after the fix: 147 org-id
entries, 7 unassignable, list returns exactly 140 — 13 had been missing.

*The pattern worth carrying forward:* both halves were assertions the
code made about itself in prose — "kept, absence of evidence rules
nothing out" — that the code did not honour. Neither was found by review.

Reported by the user 2026-08-15 after applying some events: unassigned
spans cannot be assigned because not every heading is offered by the
completion prompt, and pasting a title yields =[Match required]=.

*Reproduced by rebuilding the candidate list outside the command,*
against the six unassigned spans left in the live =*org-review*= buffer.
=claude-code-ide-org-review-assign= filters
=org-id-locations= (147 entries) and excludes any heading whose
=:CREATED:= is later than the span's end:

: span ending 15:31 -> 122 offered, 2 excluded as too new
: span ending 09:20 -> 119 offered, 5 excluded as too new

For the 09:20 span the five excluded are *exactly the five headings
created today*. So this morning's work cannot be assigned to any heading
written this afternoon, and =completing-read= is called with
=REQUIRE-MATCH= t, so a pasted title fails with no explanation of why the
heading is absent.

*The premise in the code is false.* The comment reads: "A heading created
after the span ended cannot be what that time was spent on. This is a
hard impossibility, so it is excluded outright rather than ranked down."
It is not an impossibility — doing the work first and writing the heading
afterwards is the normal case, and *this project's own convention
mandates it*: "any time a new task is described in conversation, create
an org heading for it." A heading is created when a task is described,
which is during or after the work, rarely before it. On 2026-08-15 five
headings were created for discussion that had been running since the
morning.

*The fix is already written twenty lines below, for the analogous case.*
DONE/CANCELLED headings get exactly the right treatment: "A heading
closed before the span is implausible but NOT impossible -- documenting a
task after marking it DONE is routine -- so it demotes rather than
vanishes." Created-after-span deserves the same demotion, not exclusion.
The sort key already has a slot for it.

*Ruled out while diagnosing, recorded so it is not re-checked:*

- Not a stale =org-id= index — all five of today's headings resolve in
  =org-id-locations=.
- Not the completion style — =completion-styles= is =(orderless basic)=
  with vertico, so a pasted substring would match if the candidate were
  present.
- Not the file filter, though it is worth knowing it drops 10 of 147
  (fixtures and =/tmp= scratch), and 13 more resolve to no title at all —
  stale =org-id= entries pointing at headings that no longer exist. That
  second number is its own small defect.

*Separate but adjacent:* candidate strings are decorated —
=made 08-14    [DONE] Title  {shortid}= — so the title sits mid-string.
Orderless copes, but a plain =basic= completion style would not, and the
decoration would be better as an =:affixation-function= so it is
displayed without being matched against.

*Correction 2026-08-17 to the diagnosis above.* That entry recorded, as
an aside, that "13 more resolve to no title at all -- stale =org-id=
entries pointing at headings that no longer exist. That second number is
its own small defect." Wrong on the cause. They were not stale entries.

Building the candidate list reads
=(claude-code-ide-org--parse-org-timestamp (or (org-entry-get nil "CREATED") ""))=,
and parsing the empty string *signals*. The whole =info= form is wrapped
in =ignore-errors=, so any heading without a =:CREATED:= was silently
dropped -- despite the code's own comment claiming "Headings with no
=:CREATED:= are kept -- absence of evidence rules nothing out."

Invisible in production because every heading in TODO.org carries a
=:CREATED:=; the test fixture's heading does not, which is what surfaced
it. Fixed in the same pass by guarding the parse.

*Confirmed arithmetically after the fix:* =org-id-locations= holds 147
entries, 7 of which are in unassignable files (fixtures, =/tmp=
scratch), and the candidate list now returns exactly *140*. Nothing is
dropped for any other reason. Before, 13 of those 140 were missing.

Worth keeping as a pattern rather than a one-off: an =ignore-errors=
wrapped around a *block* rather than around the one call that can
signal turns a narrow parse failure into a silent whole-record drop,
and the comment right beside it went on asserting the opposite for as
long as nobody had a fixture that hit it.

*Fixed 2026-08-17, and the fix is a deletion.* The =:CREATED:=-after-span
exclusion is gone. Nothing is filtered out of the candidate list now;
proximity ranking ([[id:49cbe319-f7db-413a-ade8-c4850c1fab8c][49cbe319]],
done in the same pass) subsumes what the exclusion was reaching for, and
does it without a cliff: a heading created five minutes after a span
scores five minutes and stays near the top, one created a week later
sorts far down on its own.

*Verified against the span that broke.* The 09:19--09:20 span of
2026-08-15 previously excluded exactly the five headings created that
day. Its candidate list now:

: 08-14..08-15  Decide whether CLAUDE.md needs a path-scoped ... {befaed0a}
: 08-06..08-15  Update CLAUDE.md and the org skill ...          {02aaae22}
: made 11:04    Make the org skill ... portable to other repos  {9ae4b17e}
: made 14:07    Record turns-per-interval ...                   {9e627dc0}
: made 14:32    Record the originating project in queue events  {83b3cdd6}

The first two overlap the span and score zero. The next three were
*unreachable* before, and are now ranked by how far after it they were
written.

*The reason this survived a 277-test suite is worth keeping.*
=claude-code-ide-org-review-assign= is =(interactive)= and calls
=completing-read=, so it had *no tests at all* -- the candidate list was
only reachable by driving a minibuffer. The fix extracts
=claude-code-ide-org--assign-candidates=, which takes a span and returns
the ranked alist, and the four new tests exercise that. Making the thing
testable was not incidental to fixing it; it is why the defect was
invisible.

All five new tests were confirmed failing first (=282 tests, 5
unexpected= before; =282, 0 unexpected= after), and the fix was reloaded
into the live Emacs and checked against real data rather than only in
batch.

Proposed by the user 2026-08-15, while hitting
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]]: the headings whose
=[CREATED]--[most recent activity]= bracket sits closest to the
unassigned interval are the most likely answers, and should float to the
top of the completion list. The bracket should also be visible, so the
human can see *why* a candidate is ranked where it is.

*Half of this already exists, which makes it cheaper than it sounds.*
=claude-code-ide-org--heading-activity-range= returns
=(EARLIEST . LATEST)= across a subtree's CLOCK lines, and
=--format-activity-range= renders it into the candidate string as a
13-character fixed-width field. Its docstring already argues this
heading's case: "A heading whose recorded work ended three days before
the span is a far weaker answer than one clocked on the same afternoon,
and neither its title nor its TODO state says so."

*What is missing is that none of it reaches the sort.* The sort key in
=claude-code-ide-org-review-assign= is, in order: DONE/CANCELLED demoted,
then most-recently-created first. Creation recency is a poor proxy —
it ranks a heading made this morning above one clocked *during the span
itself*.

*Two concrete defects to fix together:*

- *The bracket the user described is not the one computed.* They said
  =[CREATED]--[most_recent]=; the helper returns
  =[earliest CLOCK]--[latest CLOCK]= and falls back to =made MM-DD= when
  a heading has never been clocked. Those differ for exactly the headings
  that matter here: one created during the span but not yet clocked has
  no range at all. The bracket wants to be
  =[min(CREATED, earliest clock)]--[max(latest clock, CREATED)]=.
- *Day resolution is useless for same-day spans.* The field renders
  =clocked 08-15= or =clocked 08-13-08-15=. Every candidate touched today
  reads =clocked 08-15= against a span that is also 08-15, so the display
  carries no information in precisely the case a human is most often
  assigning. It needs time-of-day resolution when the bracket and the
  span share a date.

*Ranking metric, unresolved:* interval distance is the obvious choice —
zero when the bracket and the span overlap, otherwise the gap to the
nearest endpoint. Overlap is the strongest possible signal and should
dominate. Open: whether overlap *duration* should rank above mere
proximity, and how a heading with no bracket at all sorts against one
with a distant bracket — absence is not distance, and the project has
been bitten before by treating it as though it were.

*Do not let this reintroduce the exclusion in
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]].* Ranking is the
right mechanism precisely because it never removes a candidate; the bug
there is that a filter did. A heading created after the span should sort
low, not vanish.

*Implemented 2026-08-17, in one pass with
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]] since both are the
same function.*

*Three parts, matching the three defects recorded above.*

- =claude-code-ide-org--heading-bracket= merges =:CREATED:= with the
  clock extent, so the bracket is =[CREATED..latest activity]= as the
  user described rather than =[earliest clock..latest clock]=. A heading
  created during a span but never clocked now has a position instead of
  none -- which is the heading most likely to be wanted, since work
  happens and the heading is written afterwards.
- =claude-code-ide-org--interval-gap= scores proximity: zero when the
  bracket and span overlap, otherwise seconds to the nearest endpoint,
  symmetrically. Overlap dominates, which is what the "most likely
  candidate" intuition amounts to.
- =--format-activity-range= takes a reference time and renders *times*
  rather than dates when the bracket falls on the reference's day. The
  old field read =clocked 08-15= for every candidate touched today
  against a span that is also today -- no information in exactly the
  case it is most used.

Sort order is now proximity, then open-before-finished, then creation
recency. Creation recency was previously *primary*, which ranked a
heading made this morning above one clocked *during* the span.

*Verified live, against the real 14:31--15:31 span,* not only in batch:
brackets sharing the day render as =16:34..16:57= and =made 14:07=,
cross-day ones stay =08-06..08-15=.

*The open question above is resolved by construction rather than
decided:* "how does a heading with no bracket sort against one with a
distant bracket?" A heading with neither =:CREATED:= nor a CLOCK line
scores =most-positive-fixnum= and sorts last -- but is *not* removed,
because absence of evidence rules nothing out. That was the whole lesson
of 0d055205 and it would have been easy to repeat here.

*Not done:* whether overlap *duration* should outrank mere proximity.
Overlap collapses to zero either way, so a heading clocked for one minute
inside the span ties with one clocked throughout it. No evidence yet that
this matters; recorded rather than guessed at.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/plan-this-task-clever-cupcake.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation, after a frank assessment of
this whole clock/session-identity subsystem concluded the *original*
idea — Claude Code sessions driving live org state (TODO keyword,
clock, =:LOGBOOK:=/=:SESSIONS:= logging) directly and synchronously,
in real time, across concurrent/background sessions — is not bearing
fruit. Evidence, not just a vibe: in roughly a day and a half of real
use this produced two distinct clock-marker desync incidents
([[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a
live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]), a malformed bare
=CLOCK:= line from concurrent writers, an idle-clock-hijack bug
([[id:582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e][session-resume blindly
inherits any session's last-clocked heading]]), a clock-ownership
gap covering 2 of 3 clock-open paths
([[id:d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7][org_clock_in doesn't
record clock ownership, unlike session-resume]]), a zero-time-clock
erasure bug (=293ac49e=, DONE), a genuine-looking-but-actually-noise
bookkeeping delta needing human cleanup after the fact (commits
=4cc769f=/=bc45342=), and — the proximate trigger for this whole
heading — discovering that
[[id:04d0e7d5-ab6b-4972-925d-d517484c7595][native org state-change
logging]] was implemented, then found live to hang Emacs
indefinitely when triggered non-interactively via =emacsclient -e=,
and so was quietly suppressed for every tool-driven transition ever
since. Root cause, not a string of unrelated bugs: org's clock/
logging model assumes one human, one buffer, one synchronous
keypress at a time. This project's actual workload — concurrent
sessions, background agents, non-interactive =emacsclient= calls, a
global clock paused/resumed across turn boundaries — is the opposite
of that, and every incident above traces back to the same mismatch.
See [[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][Revisit the PLANNING
TODO-state's design and whether it's worth keeping]] (=MAYBE=,
captured the same day) — this heading substantially supersedes that
one's question rather than answering it in place, since a working
version of this redesign would make most of =PLANNING='s current
fragility moot.

*Proposed redesign:* Claude Code sessions stop touching the live
Emacs buffer for anything clock- or state-sensitive. Instead they
append plain, dumb, append-only JSON events to a per-session file
(e.g. =~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl=) — no buffer edits,
no clock mutation, no non-interactive logging trigger, most of it not
even requiring Emacs to be reachable to write. A new interactive
Emacs command reads the accumulated events across all pending session
files, presents them to the human as a reviewable batch (think
=org-agenda='s bulk-mark UI or =magit-status='s staging area, not a
raw diff), and applies the approved subset through org's *real*,
synchronous, single-actor machinery — =org-todo=, =org-clock-in=,
=org-clock-out= — called for real, inside a genuinely interactive
command, so native =!=/=@= logging finally just works instead of
needing to be suppressed.

*Not everything moves* — only the two categories that have actually
caused every incident: TODO-state transitions and clock start/stop.

| Stays direct, as today                                    | Moves to queue + review              |
|------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------|
| =org_query= (read-only)                                    | =org_set_todo= (all states — needed for logging to fire, not just clock states) |
| =org_clock_report= (read-only)                              | =org_clock_in= / =org_clock_out=       |
| =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling=, =org_sort_children= (never implicated in any incident) | Stop/UserPromptSubmit session-pause/resume signals (become bare timestamp appends — no Emacs call, no owner variable, no cross-session race, since there's no shared live clock left to race on) |
| =org_capture= (heading creation — separate, orthogonal bugs already noted elsewhere) | |
| =org_log_background_plan= (already deliberately clock/state-free — already exactly this pattern) | |

*What this eliminates* (mapped against the evidence above): marker
desync (no writer but the human-driven apply command ever touches
=TODO.org=/=DONE.org= — *false as written, see the correction below*),
the idle-clock-hijack and missing-ownership
bugs (moot — ownership is just the queue filename, no shared clock to
inherit), the malformed-concurrent-write =CLOCK:= corruption (exactly
one writer, one operation at a time), the noise-vs-genuine bookkeeping
judgment call (reviewed *before* anything lands, with session/timing
visible, instead of reconstructed from a git diff after the fact —
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session
bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed
session]]'s mechanism-approach likely folds into the new review UI
rather than standing alone), and the non-interactive logging hang that
started this whole thread.

*Three children were promoted out of this epic on 2026-08-13, and the
test that moved them is worth keeping.* Ask not "is this related?" —
everything here is related — but *"would this epic be incomplete without
it?"*

- [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]] carries a =+1m=
  repeater, so it never reaches =DONE=. An epic containing it can never
  be =DONE= either — and
  [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] is
  =:BLOCKER:=-ed on this epic, so one misplaced child had silently
  frozen an unrelated task. That is the concrete cost of getting this
  wrong, not a matter of taste.
- [[id:b5f94b88-76e3-4a3c-ac73-729f34d33691][b5f94b88]] has its own plan
  file, its own scope decisions and its own deferred phases. A child
  with a plan file of its own is usually an epic wearing a child's
  clothes.
- [[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] is a capability
  this epic *enables* rather than one it requires.

Everything else added late — eight defects found in this epic's own
shipped code — stayed, and rightly: discovering that your review command
fabricates intervals is not scope creep, it is the work.

They were promoted to level 2 in this category rather than gathered
under a new epic. Grouping them would have grouped by *timing* ("both
are next"), which is the same error as filing them here — proximity
mistaken for membership. An epic should earn its parenthood by having
children that must complete for it to complete; neither of those two has
children yet, and =b5f94b88= will become one naturally if its capture
and amend halves ever split.

*Correction 2026-08-13 to the marker-desync claim above, which is
load-bearing and false.* "No writer but the human-driven apply command
ever touches =TODO.org=/=DONE.org=" was never true and still is not.
=org_capture=, =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling= and
=org_sort_children= all still write those files directly and
immediately; the org skill text-edits them routinely with Claude Code's
=Edit= tool, which does not go through Emacs at all; git rewrites them
under a live buffer with =global-auto-revert-mode= on; and
=--trigger-auto-clock-in= was deliberately *kept*, so a human's own
=C-c C-t= still opens a clock.

The cutover removed the *clock and state* writers, not the *text*
writers. Marker desync is therefore not eliminated — it was observed
live 2026-08-12, and reading =org-clock.el= on 2026-08-13 showed it is
the reachable trigger for
[[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]]'s fabricated
interval, via =(error "Clock start time is gone")=. Anyone cancelling
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][53b0047d]] by citing the
sentence above would be retiring a live bug on the strength of a claim
this heading never established.

*What it costs, honestly:* real-time org state lags until a review pass runs
(mitigate by pointing the statusline at the *queue* files instead of live clock
state — see sub-task below); adds a manual review step to the human's workflow
(keep it cheap — one keybinding); and this *likely obsoletes, in their
currently-planned form*, [[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][=53b0047d='s plan]], [[file:~/.claude/plans/session-resume-continuity-guard.md][=582cc7f4='s plan]], and [[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-in-ownership-recording.md][=d150c02e='s
plan]] — each patches a problem this architecture makes structurally impossible
rather than mitigated. Not wasted work: each documents its failure mode
precisely, which is exactly the input this redesign is answering. [[file:~/.claude/plans/sessions-logbook-ordering.md][=2b0988ed='s
plan]] (entry ordering) most likely still applies independent of this refactor,
worth the evaluator's own judgment either way.

*Load-bearing assumption, explicitly flagged for early verification,
not assumed true:* this design only works if the org functions the
apply command calls (=org-clock-in=, =org-clock-out=, whatever drives
=org-add-log-note=/=org-store-log-note=) actually accept — or can be
made to accept — an explicit *retrospective* timestamp, so a batch
applied at 5pm can still backfill a =CLOCK:= interval and a state-
change log note at the times the underlying events actually happened
at 9am, 11am, etc., not "now." If that turns out to be false or only
partially true for some of these functions, significant parts of the
design below need rethinking before implementation, not after.

Nested below as separate sub-tasks, deliberately broken out for
readability rather than left as one undifferentiated blob — each gets
its own =:ID:= and starts as =TODO=; sequencing among them (which
should happen first) is left to whoever evaluates this, not fixed
here, except where a sub-task's own text says otherwise.

Triaged 2026-08-06, later the same day: they no longer all sit at
=TODO=. =c9292ce8= (read-time overlay) is =CANCELLED= as working
against this refactor's own premise; =16160d23= (per-subagent
intervals) is =MAYBE=, blocked on =3d576d29=; =7771fc63= was added for
the stale-interval-recovery feature, whose premise this design removes.
=3d576d29='s clock half is now confirmed answered and its scope
narrowed to the logging half. Each carries its own note; this is just
the index.

*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* This epic's own plan file has been
superseded on most of its concrete decisions by the two later plans, and
nothing in it says so. A reader reaching for it first — which is what an
epic-level plan invites — would be misled on the queue path, the schema,
attribution and apply bookkeeping alike:

| the plan says                                                                   | what was built                                                                |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| queue at =<repo>/.claude/org-updates/=                                          | =~/.claude/org-updates/=, global                                              |
| =session_id= as explicit tool arguments                                         | the hook writes it; the tool never sees it                                    |
| missing =session_id= lands in =unattributed.jsonl=, "never dropped"             | the event is dropped outright                                                 |
| every event carries a UUID =event_id=; =applied= events append to the same file | no =event_id= exists; applied state is a sibling =.applied= set               |
| schema =(event_id, heading_id, payload)=                                        | =(ts, kind, id, state, from, note, session_id, agent_id, agent_type, source)= |
| an elisp writer plus =bin/org-queue-append=                                     | shell only, at =bin/hooks/queue-append=                                       |
| retire =--trigger-auto-clock-in=                                                | kept, and suppressed during apply via the re-entrancy guard                   |
| branch =feature/event-queue-review=                                             | =feature/event-queue-format=                                                  |

*And one live contradiction, not merely staleness.* The plan's decision 5
records the =:SESSIONS:= drawer's retirement as *confirmed with the
user*. [[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]], written a
day later, treats the same question as deliberately deferred and
=MAYBE=. Both cannot be current, and the disagreement is load-bearing:
it decides whether a whole subsystem gets deleted.

Until the plan is revised, =event-queue-format.md= and
=review-and-cutover.md= are authoritative where they disagree with it —
they are later and were written against real code.

*Closed 2026-08-15, nine days after capture.* Final tally: 29 =DONE=
children, 2 =CANCELLED=, and 9 headings promoted out to =**= over the
epic's life rather than absorbed.

*What shipped, against what was proposed above.* The redesign landed as
described: sessions no longer touch the live buffer for clock- or
state-sensitive work. =org_set_todo= changes no keyword, =org_clock_in=
opens no clock, =org_clock_out= closes none — each appends to
=~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl= through
=bin/hooks/queue-append=, the sole writer. =M-x
claude-code-ide-org-review= reads across pending session files and
applies the approved subset through =org-todo=/=org-clock-in=/
=org-clock-out= inside a genuinely interactive command.

*The proximate trigger is resolved.* Native =!=/=@= state-change logging
was suppressed for every tool-driven transition because it hung Emacs
when driven through =emacsclient -e=. It now runs at apply time, inside a
real interactive command, and nothing is suppressed. The hang was never
fixed — it was routed around, which was the point.

*Grew well past the proposal in three directions the plan did not
anticipate:* guidepost reconstruction of spans from =pause=/=resume=
with a tuned gap threshold; span evidence at review time (commits and
=:CREATED:= stamps inside a span's window, which turned out to beat both
transcript-reading and model assistance); and permission-block
bracketing, since a turn stalled on a prompt never ends and so was
credited as work.

*Verified continuously rather than at the end:* 277 ERT tests plus four
shell suites, and real use — 23 unattributed spans assigned by hand in
one sitting, and repeated apply passes since.

*What it deliberately did not settle, and this is the honest part.* The
queue records *agent* activity and has never recorded the user's own
attention; that distinction only got its vocabulary on 2026-08-15 —
*clock intervals* measured, *attention intervals* attested — and the
second half is [[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]],
untouched. Defects found in the machinery after it shipped remain open:
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]],
[[id:49cbe319-f7db-413a-ade8-c4850c1fab8c][49cbe319]],
[[id:09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16][09c134c4]] and
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].

*Closed over two =MAYBE= children rather than by finishing them* —
[[id:118373e5-b89b-4001-ad52-1f68374745fb][118373e5]] and
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]] were promoted out
on 2026-08-15 on the reasoning this heading applied seven times before:
a capability the epic *enables* is not one it *requires*, and an epic
that keeps every downstream capability can never close. Both remain
=MAYBE= with their triggers written down.

Unblocks [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]].


** DONE Design the append-only event-queue format and per-session file lifecycle :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 22:55]--[2026-08-07 Fri 08:20] =>  9:25
- State "DOING"      from "PLANNING"   [2026-08-07 Fri 07:59] \\
  Auto-promoted: ExitPlanMode fired on the owning session.
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/event-queue-format.md][Plan]]

JSONL schema per event kind (=todo=, =clock_in=, =clock_out=,
=pause=, =resume=, at minimum), file naming/location
(=~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl= proposed, not settled),
and lifecycle after a successful apply (delete vs. truncate vs.
append an =applied= marker so a session can keep writing after a
partial drain without needing to know if it was fully consumed).
Crash-safety: a session dying mid-stream should leave its queue file
harmless and inert, never corrupt, never lost.

Note added 2026-08-06: =pause=/=resume= events are not just historical
bookkeeping — per
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s design, they
become the *guideposts* a human uses to reconstruct their own real
=CLOCK:= intervals at review time. Their timestamp density directly
determines how useful that reconstruction is, so keep emitting one
per real turn boundary (matching today's =Stop=/=UserPromptSubmit=
granularity) rather than coarsening them for this design's sake —
coarser guideposts make the human's own reconstruction job harder,
not easier.

*Open question to settle here, raised 2026-08-06: do =clock_in=/
=clock_out= events survive at all for human-attended sessions?* Follow
the design through and they are demoted from timing records to bare
*intent markers*. For a human session the interval can't be applied
from them anyway — [[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]
requires the human to draw it from =pause=/=resume= guideposts, which
are strictly denser and better-founded than a =clock_in= event. The one
place a =clock_in=/=clock_out= *pair* still carries authoritative
timing is the subagent path, and that path is now deferred
([[id:16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0][16160d23]], =MAYBE=), so
nothing in v1 consumes them as timing data at all. Two live options:
1. *Keep them as intent markers.* Their remaining job is heading
   attribution — telling the review UI which heading Claude believed it
   was on, so guideposts can be grouped when several tasks interleave
   inside one session. The guidepost stream alone does not solve that,
   which is the real argument for keeping them.
2. *Drop them for v1.* =org_set_todo= → =DOING= already carries the same
   intent, and pause/resume already carry the timing. Smaller event
   schema, and it lets CLAUDE.md's state-transition table lose the
   "always call =org_clock_in=" rule entirely, since there'd be no
   clock to open.

Leaning (1) on the interleaving argument, but genuinely undecided and
worth settling before the schema is written rather than after.

*Related, decide alongside it:* even as queue-appends, the MCP tools
still execute inside Emacs — they're served by the Emacs tools server
(=.mcp.json=, =claude-code-ide-emacs-tools-setup=), so =org_clock_in=
reaches Emacs and appends a line there. Harmless (no buffer mutation,
no clock state), but it is *not* the "no Emacs required to write"
property the parent heading claims — only the hooks get that, per
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s "no
=emacsclient= call at all." Whether the clock/state tools should also
write the queue file directly, bypassing the tools server so they work
with Emacs down, is unrecorded elsewhere and belongs to this sub-task's
file-lifecycle design.

*Both resolved during planning, 2026-08-07.* =clock_in=/=clock_out=
events are kept, on the basis the user set out: they exist to inform the
human's composition of =CLOCK:= entries at review time, contributing
only *active* timestamps to =:LOGBOOK:=, subject to aggregation at
ingestion so review stays manageable. The decisive technical argument is
attribution — =pause=/=resume= are heading-less, and a return to a
heading that never left =DOING= emits no =todo= event, so without them
every subsequent guidepost silently attributes to the wrong heading.
On the write path: the *hook* writes and the *tool* validates, since
=session_id= only ever appears on hook stdin and never reaches an MCP
tool.

*Implementation notes (2026-08-07):* shipped the schema, the writer and
the reader. =bin/hooks/queue-append= is the single shared writer, called
with the event kind as =$1= and the hook payload on stdin; it builds the
line entirely with =jq -n= (same reasoning as =session-pause='s existing
comment), refuses a =session_id= that is not a plain filename component,
and drops any =todo=/=clock_in=/=clock_out= whose =tool_response= begins
with ="Error:"= — =--at-id= returns error strings rather than throwing,
so that is the only signal a bad =:ID:= never resolved. On the Emacs
side, a new "Event queue" section adds
=claude-code-ide-org-queue-directory= and
=claude-code-ide-org-guidepost-gap-threshold= (default 15 minutes,
explicitly a starting guess) plus =--queue-events=,
=--queue-events-by-id=, =--aggregate-guideposts= and the watermark
functions. Draining never touches the =.jsonl=: a sibling
=<session-id>.applied= watermark records progress, so a session that is
still appending is never raced.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =bin/test= passes 134/134 (124
pre-existing + 10 new ERT tests) covering round-trip of all five kinds,
skipping torn/malformed/unknown-kind lines without failing the file,
watermark filtering including a session that keeps appending after a
partial drain, atomicity of the watermark write, the =A → B → A=
attribution case, cross-session isolation, gap clustering at two
thresholds, and that a 2-minute span survives where
=--consolidate-logbook-text= drops it (asserted as a test, not a
comment). =bin/queue-append-test= adds 20 shell assertions on the writer
— field mapping per kind, ="Error:"= suppression, missing/traversing
=session_id=, unparseable payload, append accumulation, per-session
files, always-exit-0. Also ran a cross-boundary pass neither suite
covers: the real hook script wrote a nine-event =A → B → A= stream which
the real Emacs reader then attributed correctly, drained 9 → 0 via the
watermark, and left the queue file intact at nine lines.

*Deviations from the plan, all deliberate:*
- =session-pause=/=session-resume= *keep* their =emacsclient= call and
  append to the queue alongside it, rather than dropping it as the plan
  said. Dropping it now would stop pausing the live clock at turn
  boundaries while the MCP tools still open real clocks — every interval
  would silently absorb idle time, with nothing yet able to apply the
  queued events instead. Additive now, removed in =feba67eb='s cutover.
- =--aggregate-guideposts= does *not* reuse
  =--merge-time-intervals= as the plan proposed: that merges only
  touching or overlapping intervals and has no gap tolerance, which is a
  different question from clustering nearby points.
- =--parse-iso8601= shape-checks its input before calling
  =date-to-time=. A test caught that =date-to-time= accepts ="nonsense"=
  and returns a near-epoch time, which would sort a garbage line *ahead*
  of every real event — corrupting ordering rather than being skipped.
- Added a generic =--atomic-write= rather than reusing
  =--write-clock-status=, which is hardwired to its own defcustom path.
  Folding the older function onto the new helper is a tidy-up left for
  whoever next touches the statusline.

*Amended 2026-08-07, after the schema was checked against
=clock-template.org=.* The original design had no =note= field, so a
drained queue would have produced bare =CLOCK:= lines and unlabelled
=State "X" from "Y"= lines — a strictly poorer =:LOGBOOK:= than the
template's conventions call for, where every non-=CLOCK:= line carries a
short label. A real gap, missed because the schema was designed against
this heading's own text rather than against the template that defines
the target output.

Added: a nullable =note= field, populated from =tool_input.note=, with
an optional =note= argument on =org_clock_in=, =org_clock_out= and
=org_set_todo= (3-10 words). The Elisp wrappers accept and *ignore* it
deliberately — writing it into the live buffer now would produce exactly
the unreviewed record this design exists to avoid, and there is no
correct place for it in a =:LOGBOOK:= until a human confirms the
interval it belongs to. At apply time it becomes the =\\= continuation
on org's native state-change line, via the =org-store-log-note= path
already confirmed in [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]]
(the note text is the note buffer's contents; empty yields a bare State
line).

Also fixed =clock_out='s null =id=. The tool takes no =id= argument —
the running clock is the only thing that knows which heading is closing
— so its reply now reports the id it *actually* closed
(="Clocked out: \"H\" (id: ID)"=) and =queue-append= recovers it from
there. Authoritative in a way a caller-supplied argument would not be:
it cannot disagree with what happened. The ="Clocked out:"= prefix is
unchanged, which is all =bin/clock-notify= matches on.

Still open, deliberately: *guidepost span labels*. In the template a
label like =- <09:00>--<09:15> clarify backend schema design= belongs to
a *span*, aggregated from many =pause=/=resume= events — and those come
from hooks Claude never invokes, so no call site can supply one. Default
plan is to inherit the enclosing =clock_in='s note, leaving spans
unlabelled where there is none, with transcript-derived labels
([[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]]) as the eventual
upgrade. Worth noting that deferral is a scope call, *not* a capability
gap: reading a transcript is plain file I/O, locatable from =session_id=
alone, and needs none of the MCP sampling machinery
=claude-code-ide= lacks.

Verified: =bin/test= 138/138 (4 new), =bin/queue-append-test= 30/30 (10
new), and =config.el= live-reloaded with the new arities and tool schema
confirmed in the running Emacs.


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* =event-queue-format.md= has drifted
from what shipped in four places:
- *The watermark design was abandoned.* The plan describes
  =<session_id>.applied= as holding "the =ts= of the last applied
  event," with everything at or before it treated as consumed. The
  implementation stores an applied *set*, because a high-water mark
  cannot advance past a skipped item — observed live 2026-08-07, when a
  real apply wrote no watermark at all and would have re-proposed
  everything.
- *It states the ="Error:"= suppression as working.* It does not, for
  the response shape a real failing MCP call produces; see
  [[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]]. The plan
  documents an intent the code does not achieve, which is worse than
  documenting nothing.
- *The schema table is missing three fields* added since: =note= (the
  amendment covers it), =agent_type=, and =from=.
- *The "no auto-rounding" rationale points at machinery that no longer
  exists.* Rounding was retired outright in
  [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]; the conclusion
  still holds but the reason cited for it, and the =config.el:900/991=
  line references, do not.


** DONE Rework the MCP tool / hook write paths to append-only :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-11 Tue 16:41]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:BLOCKER:  ids(720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 14:56]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:01] =>  0:05
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:05]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:06] =>  0:01
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:09]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:12] =>  0:03
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:16]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:19] =>  0:03
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:25]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:26] =>  0:01
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:31]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:32] =>  0:01
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:41]--[2026-08-11 Tue 15:47] =>  0:06
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 15:50]--[2026-08-11 Tue 16:03] =>  0:13
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 16:13]--[2026-08-11 Tue 16:59] =>  0:46
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 13:49]--[2026-08-11 Tue 13:55] =>  0:06
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 16:41] \\
  cutover verified by the first post-cutover session
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 13:29]--[2026-08-11 Tue 13:46] =>  0:17
- State "NEXT"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 12:26] \\
  deferred to a fresh session, order recorded
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-11 Tue 12:14] \\
  blocker cleared, starting the cutover
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 12:25]--[2026-08-11 Tue 12:26] =>  0:01

- [2026-08-11 Tue 14:53]--[2026-08-11 Tue 16:59]

[[file:~/.claude/plans/review-and-cutover.md][Plan]]

Planned 2026-08-07 jointly with
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]] as one two-phase
plan (this heading is Phase B); see that heading for why they share one
plan file. The =:BLOCKER:= is not bookkeeping: the moment these tools
stop mutating live state, something has to apply the queued events, and
that something is =720b2dcf=. Landing this first would leave every
transition and interval queued with no way to reach an org file.

*Settled during planning:* the tool-side guards are *retired* rather
than made queue-aware — =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= and
=bin/clock-notify= (with =bin/clock-notify-test=) both exist to police
live state against concurrent writers, and after the cutover there are
none. Human review becomes the validation step, which is the point of
the refactor. The org-side hooks (=org-blocker-hook=/=org-trigger-hook=)
stay: they still guard genuine hand-edits made directly in Emacs.

=org_set_todo=, =org_clock_in=, =org_clock_out= become queue-appends
instead of live buffer mutations (per the routing table above).
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= become bare timestamp
appends with no =emacsclient= call at all — this alone is what
retires the =337f7fb2=/=582cc7f4=/=d150c02e= ownership-variable
infrastructure, so double-check nothing else still depends on
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id=/
=--planning-owner-session-id= before deleting them.

Three existing =PostToolUse=/=PreToolUse= hooks don't just port over
unchanged and are easy to overlook since they aren't in the routing
table above (that table is about MCP tools, not the hook layer
sitting alongside them):
- =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= validates a transition
  against the heading's *current* state before allowing it — but
  "current" on disk goes stale the moment anything for that heading
  is queued-but-unapplied. Needs to become queue-aware (validate
  against the latest *queued* state for that heading, not disk
  state) or it will wrongly allow/reject transitions once a queue
  exists.
- =bin/clock-notify= exists to cross-check a tool's claimed clock
  result against live =(org-clocking-p)=. Once =org_clock_in=/
  =org_clock_out= never touch live clock state at all, that
  comparison is meaningless — retire it or repurpose it into
  something like "you have N unreviewed updates" instead of "clock
  state mismatch."
- Three clock settings in the user's *personal Doom config*
  (=~/.config/doom/config.el=), added 2026-08-06 — outside this repo,
  which is exactly why they're easy to miss when sweeping the repo for
  clock callers. All three become dead code, because after this
  refactor a clock is only ever live for the microseconds between the
  paired =org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= calls inside the apply command:
  =claude-code-ide-org--clock-out-if-clocking= on =kill-emacs-hook= and
  on =suspend-hook= (config.el:88-91) exist to catch a clock left
  running across a quit or suspend — =(org-clocking-p)= will
  essentially never be true at those moments any more; and
  =org-clock-out-when-done t= (config.el:67), which fires on =org-todo=
  → =DONE=, now only ever runs inside apply where the clock is already
  closed. Removing them is optional (they're harmless no-ops), but
  leaving them in place misleads the next reader into thinking a
  background clock still exists.
- =bin/hooks/exitplanmode-promote-planning= auto-promotes =PLANNING=
  → =DOING= by reusing the same live clock interval. Under this
  design that becomes just another queued =todo= event stacked on
  the still-unapplied =PLANNING= one — fine, but it means the
  apply-time reconciliation (see the review-and-apply command below)
  has to correctly collapse a =TODO→PLANNING→DOING= event sequence
  into one coherent interval, not replay each event as if
  independent. Concrete supporting case for why the backdated-
  timestamp sub-task below matters as much as it does.


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* Phase B of =review-and-cutover.md=
inherits the stale watermark and rounding passages noted on
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]], and has a
structural weakness of its own: it is a *list of edits*, not a sequenced
plan. Phase A gets ordering, hook interactions and failure modes; Phase B
gets bullet points. For the epic's single behavior-changing commit — the
one whose rollback story is "git revert" — that asymmetry is worth
correcting before execution rather than during it.

Specifically unaddressed by the bullets: what order the edits land in so
the suite stays green between them, and what the first session after the
cutover is expected to look like, which is the only real check that the
write paths are all actually severed.

*Working order, derived 2026-08-11 before the first edit.* Not a
re-plan: =review-and-cutover.md='s Phase B decisions are settled and
still hold. What it lacked was an order, which is what follows. One of
its bullets is sequenced wrong, and that is the interesting part.

*The guards must be retired WITH the wrappers, not after.* The plan lists
them as separate bullets, which reads as "whenever." Both become
actively harmful the moment the tools stop mutating:
- =pretooluse-transition-guard= validates a requested transition against
  the heading's *current disk state*. Once a transition only queues, disk
  stops moving — so after queueing =TODO -> DOING=, the guard still sees
  =TODO= and would *reject* the following =DOING -> DONE= as illegal. It
  does not merely go stale; it starts blocking correct work.
- =clock-notify= cross-checks a tool's claimed clock result against live
  =(org-clocking-p)=. Once =org_clock_in= never opens a clock, that
  comparison reports a mismatch on *every* call.

*And session-pause/resume must come after the wrappers, not before.* If
they drop their =emacsclient= call while the tools still open real
clocks, nothing pauses at a turn boundary and every interval inflates
with idle time — the exact reason the additive step was taken during
[[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]] instead of the
plan's original one-shot.

So:

1. *Wrappers + guards, together.* =org_set_todo=/=org_clock_in=/
   =org_clock_out= validate the =:ID:= and return "queued, pending
   review" without touching a buffer; delete
   =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard=, =bin/clock-notify= and
   =bin/clock-notify-test= with their =.claude/settings.json= entries.
   Leaves clocking inert, which is correct — apply is the writer now.
2. *=session-pause=/=session-resume=* drop =emacsclient= and become bare
   =queue-append= wrappers.
3. *=exitplanmode-promote-planning=* queues a =todo= event instead of
   driving elisp. Sequenced here because it is the case that most needs
   apply to collapse a =TODO -> PLANNING -> DOING= run into one interval
   rather than replaying three.
4. *Ownership variables* and their machinery
   (=--clock-owner-session-id=, =--planning-owner-session-id=,
   =--maybe-record-planning-owner=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=). Last
   because steps 2 and 3 are what remove their readers; deleting earlier
   would break code still calling them.

Tests move with each step rather than after: their assertions invert from
"the buffer and clock changed" to "an event was appended and the buffer
is untouched," and a suite left asserting the old contract in between
would be noise rather than a signal.

Still one commit, per the plan — the order above is how the edits are
made and the suite is run, not a sequence of commits. =git revert= as
rollback only works if the cutover is atomic.

*Executed 2026-08-11, in the recorded order, one working tree.*

1. *Wrappers + guards.* =org_clock_in= validates the =:ID:= and returns
   ="Queued clock_in on \"H\"; pending review."=; =org_clock_out= returns
   a constant and takes no =:ID:= at all; =org_set_todo= validates the
   =:ID:= *and* the keyword against =org-todo-keywords-1= (buffer-local,
   so it checks what the file declares rather than a list hard-coded in
   the wrapper) and returns ="Queued todo -> DOING (was NEXT): ...="=.
   Deleted =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard=, =bin/clock-notify=,
   =bin/clock-notify-test= and their =settings.json= entries.
2. *=session-pause=/=session-resume=* are now one =exec= into
   =queue-append= each, with no =emacsclient= call.
3. *=exitplanmode-promote-planning=* reads the session's own queue file
   for the heading it most recently queued =PLANNING= on and appends a
   =DOING= event beside it.
4. *Ownership variables* (=--log-session-id=, =--clock-owner-session-id=,
   =--planning-owner-session-id=) and their machinery
   (=--maybe-record-planning-owner=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=,
   =bin/hooks/posttooluse-record-planning-owner=) deleted.

*Three things went beyond the recorded order, each because leaving them
would have left something that lies:*
- *The one-minute clock floor* (=--minimum-clock-seconds=,
  =--clock-out-floor-time=) went with =clock_out='s body. It was a
  write-path policy and apply is the write path now — and apply
  deliberately writes exact confirmed endpoints, so a floor would be the
  rounding this epic already retired once.
- *The three MCP tool descriptions* — model-facing text still promising
  "Opens a CLOCK entry and starts the Emacs clock timer." Every one now
  says the event is queued and the file does not change, because the
  description is the only thing telling a session not to expect a later
  read to reflect its own call.
- *=queue-append= grew explicit =QUEUE_APPEND_ID/STATE/FROM= overrides*,
  for events with no tool call behind them. The alternative — having
  =exitplanmode= synthesise a fake =tool_response= for the existing sed
  to recover — was rejected as unreadable.

*=clock_out= no longer reports an =:ID:=, and does not need to.* It used
to recover the running clock's heading so =queue-append= could sed it
out of the reply. =--queue-events-by-id= already attributes a null-id
=clock_out= to whichever heading that session's last =clock_in= named,
which is strictly better: the only clock that can be running now belongs
to the *human*, so reading one would attribute a session's interval to
whatever the user happens to be clocking.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 145/145, =bin/queue-append-test= green (12 new
checks, including that an override beats =tool_input= and that both
post-cutover reply shapes still parse), =bin/exitplanmode-promote-test=
green — a *new* harness, 15 checks. That last one is worth naming: the
ExitPlanMode hook had no automated coverage before and could not
reasonably have had any, being a here-doc of elisp handed to
=emacsclient=; it is now a bash read of one queue file, and the
cross-session guard that used to need an in-memory ownership variable is
tested as what it now is — structural, since the file is per-session.

*The test suite inverted rather than shrank.* 28 tests failed on the
first run after the wrappers changed, every one of them a test that used
a wrapper as *setup* ("clock in", "make it DOING") rather than testing
its contract. Two helpers —
=claude-code-ide-org-test--clock-in-for-real= and =--set-todo-for-real=
— now say so explicitly, which makes the remaining wrapper tests
unambiguous: they assert nothing happened. Net 168 → 145 ERT tests: 16
deleted with the code they covered (session pause/resume ownership,
PLANNING promotion, the clock floor, the clock_out id), the rest
rewritten in place.

*Live-verified against the real =TODO.org=, not just batch.* Reloaded
=config.el= and unbound the ten deleted symbols by hand (=load-file=
evaluates what is in a file; it does not unbind what was removed from
it). Then called all three wrappers on this very heading: each returned
its "pending review" string, =set_todo "WAIT"= reported =(was DOING)=
while the heading *stayed* =DOING=, an undeclared keyword and a bad
=:ID:= both came back =Error:=-prefixed — the prefix =queue-append= drops
on — and the file's sha256 was byte-identical before and after with the
buffer unmodified.

*One piece of pre-cutover state was cleaned up deliberately:* the clock
this session opened on this heading at 13:29, before the wrappers
stopped clocking. Nothing would ever have closed it — the =Stop= hook is
queue-only as of this commit — so it was closed through =org-clock-out=,
which is exactly what the old hook would have done at that moment.
Left open it would have tripped the stale-interval recovery check
tomorrow and blocked this heading from reaching =DONE=.

*Not claimed, and the reason this heading is not DONE:* the first
post-cutover session. Everything above is batch, shell, and
=emacsclient=; the plan's own gate for this work is "what the first
session after the cutover looks like," and that cannot happen inside the
session that made the change — =.claude/settings.json= is read once at
session start, so *this* session is still running the old hook wiring,
pointing at three scripts that no longer exist. The first real check is
a fresh session: its =SessionStart=/=UserPromptSubmit= hooks fire the new
scripts, its tool calls queue instead of mutating, and a review pass
applies them.

*Gate met 2026-08-11. Closing.* The next session began 95 seconds after
the cutover commit (=220ba78= 13:50:24, =SessionStart= 13:51:59) and
exercised all three clauses:

- *Hooks fired the new scripts.* Every =UserPromptSubmit= and =Stop= in
  that session produced exactly one =resume=/=pause= in the queue, with
  no spurious or missing entries, matched one-for-one against the
  transcript's own prompt and =stop_hook_summary= records.
- *Tool calls queued instead of mutating.* =org_clock_in=,
  =org_clock_out= and =org_set_todo= were each called against live
  headings; TODO.org's sha256 was unchanged across all of them.
- *A review pass applied them.* Two state transitions were confirmed and
  written with native =State= lines, the watermark advanced by exactly
  those two timestamps, and the =.jsonl= was left intact.

*What the gate caught, which is the point of having had one.* The
session found a live escape of
[[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]] — a =clock_in= for
a nonexistent =:ID:= entering the queue, because the =Error:= guard
could not see a content-block =tool_response=. Fixed and closed. Its
downstream damage was then observed for real at the review pass: the
phantom captured a guidepost interval, the heading actually being worked
on recorded nothing, and the 205 seconds proposed were idle time. None
of that was reachable from batch.

*No =clock_out= was queued when closing this heading, deliberately.* The
transition rule that =DOING -> DONE= closes the clock was written for the
live-clocking model. There is no live clock to close; the =clock_in=
queued against this heading at 15:10:36 is orphaned — it produced no
review item at all — and a =clock_out= appended now would be captured by
the same =dead-beef= cursor, manufacturing a fourth permanently
un-appliable queue item. Recorded rather than worked around, since
"what closing a heading means when the clock is a queued concept" is a
real gap in the transition rules that
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] should settle.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/review-and-cutover.md][Plan]]

Planned 2026-08-07 jointly with
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] as one two-phase
plan (this heading is Phase A), because the cutover cannot land until
this command works and most of its requirements fall out of this
design. Both headings link the same plan file — a deliberate,
one-off departure from one-plan-per-heading, taken because two
documents that must agree with each other is the worse failure mode.

The core deliverable: scan pending queue files, reconstruct proposed
per-heading transitions and clock intervals, render a review buffer
(mark/unmark, jump-to-heading, edit-an-interval, apply-marked), and
on apply call the real =org-todo=/=org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= one
item at a time, synchronously, inside this one command — genuinely
interactive, so native logging fires without the =org-inhibit-
logging= workaround.

*Core design principle, added 2026-08-06 from conversation: two
genuinely different modes, not one uniform "propose an interval"
behavior* — the system cannot tell when a human was actually
reading/thinking/composing versus idle, attending to something else
entirely (another task, email, lunch), between logged interaction
points, so it must not pretend otherwise:
- *Unattended/subagent-derived events*
  ([[id:16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0][16160d23]]) — the
  interval genuinely *is* knowable (a subagent was either running or
  not; =duration_ms= is authoritative), so the apply command can
  propose, and the human can accept in bulk, a real =CLOCK:= entry
  directly. Still reviewed, per the core queue+review model — but
  mechanically derived, not reconstructed.
- *Human-session-derived events* — the apply command must *not*
  auto-write a =CLOCK:= interval on the human's behalf; doing so
  would be exactly the kind of "confidently wrong" record this whole
  redesign exists to avoid. Instead: render each logged =pause=/
  =resume= interaction timestamp
  ([[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]]) as an
  *active*-timestamp annotation line alongside the heading's =:LOGBOOK:=
  — a "guidepost," not a claim of attention — and leave the actual
  =CLOCK:= interval for the human to draw/type/adjust themselves. A
  suggested default (e.g. span from first to last guidepost in a
  tightly-clustered run) is fine to offer, but must be clearly
  editable/rejectable, never silently committed as fact.

This is also the concrete mechanism for the active-vs-inactive
timestamp convention captured the same day: an *active* annotation
(=<start>--<end> label=) means "a human was logged interacting with
this span, worth a glance in the agenda"; the =CLOCK:= line itself
stays *inactive* as org already does it, unchanged, still the sole
arithmetic source of truth. This lets org's own agenda double as a
free, retrospective "what did I actually attend to" view, no bespoke
code needed for that part — see
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]] for the
verification step this leans on before being trusted.

*Implementation notes (2026-08-07):* =claude-code-ide-org-review= opens
a =*org-review*= buffer in a =special-mode=-derived major mode, grouped
by heading, with =m=/=u=/=t= marking, =e= to edit an interval, =RET= to
jump, =x= to apply marked, =g= to refresh. Items are built by
=--review-items-from-queue=, which classifies the two modes: a
subagent's own paired =clock_in=/=clock_out= becomes an authoritative
interval, while human =pause=/=resume= guideposts cluster into a
=:suggested= span labelled from the enclosing =clock_in='s note.
Applying is =--review-apply-item=, one item at a time.

Three things the apply path gets right on purpose, each traceable to a
specific earlier finding:
- It binds =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active=, this module's
  own existing re-entrancy guard, to suppress
  =--trigger-auto-clock-in=. Without it the trigger opens a clock at
  *now* on any → DOING/PLANNING transition, corrupting a backdated
  apply.
- It calls *raw* =org-clock-out=, never
  =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out=, so =consolidate-history='s 5-minute
  rounding never touches a confirmed interval.
- It binds =--log-source= to ="org_review_apply"=, so audit records are
  attributed rather than defaulting to ="hand-edit"= — which matters
  more once apply is the only writer of live state (see
  [[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]]).

*Two findings from verification, both of which changed the code:*
- The note-clobber symptom observed live during
  [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] does *not*
  reproduce under =emacs --batch= — the note lands fine there. What
  does reproduce, and is environment-independent, is the stray
  =CLOCK:= at *now*. The regression test asserts that instead, and says
  in its own docstring why it deliberately covers less than the live
  symptom.
- Run against the real 30-event queue, a lone unbracketed guidepost
  produced a zero-width span, which would have written
  =CLOCK: ... =>  0:00=. Now a zero-width span is annotated but writes
  no CLOCK line: one interaction point is not a duration. Distinct from
  the rounding this design rejects — nothing is discarded, because
  there was no interval to discard.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 149/149 (11 new) covering exact backdated
endpoints, a surviving 2-minute interval, active-vs-inactive annotation
per mode, the backdated state line with its note continuation, trigger
suppression both ways, item classification, contiguous-only watermark
advance, clock-then-DONE not tripping the blocker hook, and an
unresolvable =:ID:= being reported rather than thrown. Live-reloaded and
rendered against the real queue: items classify correctly and heading
titles resolve.

*Still owed:* an interactive by-hand pass — marking and applying from
the buffer against a real heading. =3d576d29= established that the
logging path behaves differently under =emacsclient -e= than in batch,
so nothing above proves the interactive apply works end to end. That is
the gate before [[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]].


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* Phase A of =review-and-cutover.md=
carries the same two drifts as the schema plan — it describes the
watermark as advancing "only to the last *contiguous* applied event"
when the implementation stores an applied set, and it justifies "no
rounding" by pointing at =consolidate-history= machinery that
[[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] has since removed.

*More consequentially, one item in the plan was silently dropped in
implementation.* The plan's own rendering mockup includes a line reading
=unattributed: 2 guideposts=. Nothing in the command displays those; the
nil-keyed group is skipped whole. Surfacing them is now tracked as
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]], which should be
read as a *regression against this plan* rather than as a new idea —
it was designed in from the start.

*Gate met, 2026-08-11.* The "still owed" above was an interactive
by-hand pass, on the grounds that
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] showed the logging
path behaves differently under =emacsclient -e= than in batch. It has now
happened three times, and the command survived all three:

- *2026-08-07*, the first pass, which exposed five bugs. Four were fixed
  that day; the fifth was
  [[id:3d93021d-1d8a-4657-bc5c-77734f91fb9d][3d93021d]], root-caused to
  stale global =org-log-note-marker= state and fixed with a one-line
  clear whose reasoning is now a comment.
- *2026-08-10*, a clock item applied cleanly: the reviewed span landed in
  =:LOGBOOK:= with active timestamps and an exact =0:01= CLOCK line. That
  interval was destroyed minutes later, which found
  [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] — a bug in
  consolidation, not in apply.
- *2026-08-11*, a real batch of state items, which exercised the
  stale-transition guard in the field. It fired on all four, as it should
  have; they were confirmed at the prompt, which is the override behaving
  as designed.

*What this heading does not close, deliberately.* Two known gaps have
their own headings rather than holding this one open:
- [[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] — unattributed
  guideposts are dropped rather than surfaced. Read that as a *regression
  against this heading's own plan*, whose rendering mockup includes an
  =unattributed: 2 guideposts= line, rather than as a new idea.
- [[id:d409390b-6624-4820-82bb-730d763962fd][d409390b]] — three
  interaction fixes requested after using it for real: mark advances,
  the note is editable, and a dired-style mark-all.

Neither blocks [[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]:
the cutover needs a command that applies queued events correctly, which
this is, not a comfortable one.


** DONE Root-cause how apply wrote a DONE nobody queued, onto the wrong heading :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       3d93021d-1d8a-4657-bc5c-77734f91fb9d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 19:40]
:BLOCKER:  ids(720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1)
:END:
*The most serious thing the 2026-08-07 interactive apply did, and the
mechanism is NOT yet understood.* Recorded with that uncertainty intact
rather than a plausible story, because a wrong explanation here is worse
than none.

*What is established, by inspection:*
- The queue contains exactly three =todo= events: =32272061 → DONE=
  (08:19), =720b2dcf → PLANNING= (11:51), =720b2dcf → DOING= (16:06).
  *None* names =feba67eb=.
- After the apply, =feba67eb= was =DONE=, carrying
  =- State "DONE" from "NEXT" [2026-08-07 Fri 08:19]= — stamped with
  *32272061's* event timestamp, and placed *outside* =:LOGBOOK:=, which
  is apply's signature under the =org-log-into-drawer= bug fixed the
  same day.
- =32272061=, the event's actual target, has *no* =DONE= state line at
  all — consistent with =org-todo "DONE"= being a no-op there, since it
  was already =DONE=.
- No trigger hook can produce this: the only =org-todo= calls in the
  trigger layer set ="DOING"=, ="TODO"= and ="NEXT"=. No heading carries
  a =:TRIGGER:= property, so =org-depend-trigger-todo= is not involved
  either.

*Two candidate mechanisms, neither confirmed:*
- =org-log-note-marker= surviving between items within a single apply.
  =org-store-log-note= is driven directly for each item; if a previous
  item's marker or =org-log-note-state=/=-previous-state= is still set
  when the next item runs, a note can be written at the wrong place with
  the wrong content. The leaked =post-command-hook= (fixed same day)
  makes this family of state-leak plausible.
- Mis-resolution in =claude-code-ide-org--at-id=, which uses
  =(org-id-find id 'marker)=. Less likely — =org-id-find-id-in-file=
  does a real property search rather than trusting a cached position —
  but not ruled out.

*Why this outranks everything else in the epic:* the failure is silent
and the output is plausible. A heading was marked complete, with a
correctly-formatted log line citing a real timestamp, and nothing
errored. That is precisely the "confidently wrong record"
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] exists to
eliminate — produced by the machinery built to eliminate it.

Repaired by hand: =feba67eb= restored to =TODO= (its state at commit
=c81cba6=, before the apply) and both spurious state lines removed.

*ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED 2026-08-07.* Reproduced deterministically, and it
was the first candidate above — stale global note state, though by a
more specific route than "leaks between items":

=org-todo= calls =org-add-log-setup= *only when the state actually
changes*. Applying a state item whose heading already holds the
requested state is a no-op that sets up no note at all. But
=org-log-note-marker=, =org-log-note-state= and
=org-log-note-previous-state= are *global* and persist from whatever
last touched them — including a different heading in a different file.
=--review-apply-state= then drove =org-store-log-note= unconditionally,
which faithfully wrote that stale note at that stale marker.

Which is exactly what the live symptoms said, once read carefully: the
=32272061 → DONE= event was a no-op (that heading was already =DONE=,
and indeed has no =DONE= log line to this day), so nothing was set up;
the marker still pointed at =feba67eb=, with state/previous-state left
as ="DONE"=/="NEXT"= — producing a correctly-formatted line, on the
wrong heading, citing a real timestamp, with no error anywhere.

*Fix:* clear =org-log-note-marker= immediately *before* calling
=org-todo=, so the post-call check answers "did *this* call set up a
note?" rather than "has anything, ever?". One line, but the reasoning
belongs in the comment, which it now has.

*Verified:* two new tests — the isolated mechanism (prime the marker at
a bystander heading in another file, apply a no-op transition, assert
the bystander is untouched), and the literal live shape (a batch whose
first item is a no-op followed by a real transition, asserting no
leakage between items and that the real one lands where it belongs).
Both fail against the previous code. =bin/test= 157/157.

*Not claimed:* that apply is now safe to run unattended against real
files. This was one bug of five found by a single interactive pass, and
[[id:f9f61c04-150b-4ee7-96c9-582cf2bda95a][f9f61c04]] — replaying state
events reality has moved past — is still open and still writes
plausible-looking wrong history.


** DONE Apply must not replay a state event that reality has moved past :code:
