Discovered 2026-08-06 while committing the
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] Plan link
(commit =727d94a=): =claude-code-ide-org--consolidate-logbook-text=
rebuilds a =:LOGBOOK:= body from parsed =CLOCK:= lines *only* — its
=--parse-clock-lines= keeps an open line and closed intervals and
nothing else — so every other line in the drawer is silently
discarded on each clock-out's consolidation pass. Live casualty,
caught in that commit's own diff: the epic heading's *native*
state-change note

: - State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 12:07] \\
:   Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.

was destroyed by the Stop-hook clock-out at 16:50. Restore that exact
line as part of the fix — restoring it any earlier is futile, since
the very next clock-out wipes it again.

Fix shape: preserve non-CLOCK lines verbatim through consolidation
(position among the rebuilt CLOCK lines is cosmetic; survival is
not). Directly load-bearing for
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]: the
review-and-apply command's whole point is writing native state-change
notes and guidepost annotations into =:LOGBOOK:= — worthless if the
next consolidation eats them. Also fresh, concrete evidence for
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][782cda6c]] (bookkeeping
noise audit before committing): the noise this time contained silent
data *loss*, not just clutter.

*Verified, not just implemented:* both consolidators now carry
every unrecognized line through verbatim —
=--parse-clock-lines=/=--parse-session-lines= gained an =:other=
channel, emitted after the rebuilt CLOCK block (=:LOGBOOK:=) and
between the collapsed days and the open tail (=:SESSIONS:=). Two
new ERT regressions reproduce the incident's exact drawer shape
(124/124 green via =bin/test=), and after a live reload the
destroyed State "NEXT" note was restored on
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] and survived
a real in-situ =consolidate-history= pass. Scope grew one sibling
during the fix: =:SESSIONS:= consolidation had the same bug class
and was silently eating =Background-planned= write-backs; fixed
and regression-tested in the same pass.


* DONE Concurrent Claude Code sessions share one global org-clock with no session identity :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       337f7fb2-b9e9-4c02-82dd-d88e60df364b
:CREATED:  [2026-07-30 Thu 12:39]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-08-17 Mon 17:13
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Clock lifecycle & visibility
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-07-30 Thu 17:30]--[2026-07-30 Thu 17:40] =>  0:10
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/concurrent-session-clock-identity.md][Plan]]

Surfaced 2026-07-30 while starting work on the sibling entry above
(=session-resume= reopening a DONE heading's clock, ID
2e34162a-4720-4d23-bf29-10eb295cd087), with a second concurrent
session active on the Warp MCP proxy bridge task (ID
6a6d5b4e-0327-4578-a44a-356576870ceb). The =Stop=/=UserPromptSubmit=
hooks (=session-pause=/=session-resume=) operated on the single global
Emacs clock and =org-clock-history= with no notion of which session
they belong to — any concurrent session's turn boundary could pause,
resume, or steal another session's running clock.

*Verified, not just implemented:* threaded Claude Code's own
=session_id= (already present on every Stop/UserPromptSubmit hook
payload, previously discarded via =cat >/dev/null=) through
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= — via =jq='s own
JSON-string encoding into the =emacsclient -e= call, not naive shell
interpolation, avoiding any injection risk — down to new optional
=session-id= arguments on
=claude-code-ide-org-session-pause=/=-resume=. A new
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id= records which session's
=session-resume= call most recently opened the running clock;
=session-pause=/=session-resume= now no-op instead of
pausing/stealing a clock owned by a different session. =nil=
session-id (a manual/test call, or omitted from the hook payload)
reproduces the exact original unconditional behavior — not a special
case, the correct fallback for the ordinary single-session case.
=:SESSIONS:= entries also record the session-id when known, directly
visible evidence for verification. Explicitly out of scope:
=clock-status.json= has the same latent single-global-file gap under
concurrent writes — not fixed here.

Deliberately does not attempt true concurrent clocking (org itself
only ever supports one running clock); narrows the failure mode to the
same low-cost "stray interval, self-corrects" category already
accepted for the unrelated "wrong but still active task" case.

=bin/test= passes 85/85 (5 new regression tests: cross-session pause
and resume both correctly blocked and leave the other session's clock
untouched; same-session pause still succeeds; omitting session-id
still bypasses the ownership check entirely; the :SESSIONS: session-id
annotation is present). All 5 pre-existing session-pause/-resume tests
pass unchanged. Also verified live end-to-end through the actual bash
hook scripts (not just the elisp functions directly) against a scratch
heading: simulated two concurrent sessions via fabricated
Stop/UserPromptSubmit JSON payloads piped into
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= — session B's pause and
resume attempts on session A's running clock both correctly no-op'd
with the clock left untouched and still owned by A; session A then
paused its own clock normally, with the owner correctly clearing
afterward.

:ID:       293ac49e-3324-421c-8495-de1a46fc04ac
:CREATED:  [2026-07-30 Thu 14:50]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-08-17 Mon 17:13

CLOCK: [2026-07-30 Thu 17:25]--[2026-07-30 Thu 17:30] =>  0:05
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/zero-time-clock-erasure.md][Plan]]

Found live: the sibling heading above (=session-resume= reopening a
DONE heading's clock, ID 2e34162a-4720-4d23-bf29-10eb295cd087) did
real, substantial work — a new function, a regression test — but its
=:LOGBOOK:= was completely empty. Root cause: Doom's =lang/org= module
sets =org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks t= (not anything this
project configured); org-native =org-clock-out= deletes a just-closed
=CLOCK:= line outright based on its *raw* duration, before
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= (this project's own
rounding+merge+drop logic, which already replicates the same intent
correctly) ever gets a chance to run.

*Verified, not just implemented:* fixed precisely at the conflict
point, not globally — =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= now let-binds
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= to nil around its own
=(org-clock-out)= call, so org-native's premature deletion never fires
on the Claude-tool/hook-driven path, while a human's own manual
=org-clock-out= keeps org's normal behavior untouched. New regression
test (=claude-code-ide-org-test-clock-out-suppresses-zero-time-removal=)
captures the dynamic value =org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks=
actually has at the moment org-clock.el's own =org-clock-out= runs,
proving it's nil there even with the test's outer binding (simulating
Doom's real override) set to t — direct mechanism verification, since
interval-arithmetic analysis showed a same-minute (the only case org's
raw check fires on) interval rounds to zero either way at minute
resolution, so asserting on final disk content alone can't distinguish
buggy from fixed. =bin/test= passes 80/80. Also confirmed live against
the running Doom Emacs (reload, then the same dynamic-binding capture
against a scratch heading): a same-minute clock-in/out under
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= t completes cleanly with the
interval correctly dropped by this project's own logic, no crash, no
premature deletion.


* DONE Enforce the transition rules :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       39b08ecd-6350-48a2-b169-98535b626ac7
:CREATED:  [2026-07-30 Thu 15:56]

CLAUDE.md's transition table (TODO→DOING opens a clock, etc.) was pure
prose at one point, with nothing stopping a hand-edit in Emacs, or a
future tool call, from violating it. Recreated 2026-07-30 as a =NEXT=
after being found stripped out of TODO.org's own cleanup with no
subheadings ever added under it — but turned out to already be
implemented and tested in =config.el=/=config-test.el=, just never
reflected back into the roadmap.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p=
(on =org-blocker-hook=) denies a DONE transition while that exact
heading's own clock is running, and
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= (on =org-trigger-hook=)
auto-opens a clock the instant any heading becomes DOING — both
registered globally via =with-eval-after-load 'org=, so they catch
every path, not just =claude-code-ide-org-set-todo=. Confirmed the
"Claude-side hook, one step earlier" half of the original design was
never a separate mechanism to build:
=claude-code-ide-org-set-todo= calls plain =org-todo=, which *is* the
function these hooks intercept, so Claude's own tool path already goes
through the same enforcement as a hand-edit — no narrower catch point
exists to add. =config-test.el= has four dedicated regression tests,
including one that bypasses the wrapper entirely (a bare =org-todo=
call) to prove the enforcement lives in org itself. =bin/test= passes
79/79.


* DONE session-resume can reopen a clock on an already-DONE heading :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       2e34162a-4720-4d23-bf29-10eb295cd087
:CREATED:  [2026-07-29 Wed 14:17]

Caught live in this session: =org-clock-in-last= (called by
=session-resume= on =UserPromptSubmit=) resumes onto
=org-clock-history='s most recent entry with no check that it's still
active — so finishing a task doesn't stop it from being re-clocked on
the next turn boundary if nothing else clocks in after. Recurred three
times in a row on the same just-finished heading — once per turn
boundary, until a heading existed here to track it as work rather than
just stripping the stray entry again; see the commit that fixed
org-agenda-files for context on what was being worked on when this was
first found.

*Verified, not just implemented:* added
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-history-head-done-p=, which checks whether
=(car org-clock-history)= now points to a DONE-type heading, and a
guard clause in =claude-code-ide-org-session-resume= that short-circuits
with a "already DONE; nothing to resume" message instead of calling
=org-clock-in-last= when that's true. The separate, deliberately-kept
behavior — resuming onto the wrong but still-*active* task, which
self-corrects via the next =org_clock_in= — is untouched. =bin/test=
passes 79/79 including a new regression test
(=session-resume-noop-when-history-head-done=) that pauses a clocked
task, marks it DONE, and asserts resume refuses to reopen it. Also
verified live against the running Doom Emacs (reload + a scratch-file
functional check mirroring the unit test, not the real tracked
headings) after reconnecting a mid-task Emacs/MCP-server outage that
interrupted this session. While starting this task, surfaced a
separate, previously-untracked gap — two concurrent Claude Code
sessions share one global org-clock with no session identity — spun
off as its own heading below rather than folded into this fix.


* DONE Auto-consolidate SESSIONS/LOGBOOK on the fly :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:CREATED:  [2026-07-28 Tue 13:13]
:ID:       a36d2436-7601-4bf5-a2e7-a4613755156d
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-07-29 Wed 08:12
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Roadmap/Clock lifecycle & visibility
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-07-28 Tue 16:50]--[2026-07-28 Tue 17:00] =>  0:10
:END:
Companion to the retrospective one-time cleanup ("Consolidate
historical SESSIONS/LOGBOOK entries", done this session): wire the
same rounding/merging logic into =org_clock_out= itself, so the
drawers stay consolidated continuously instead of needing another
manual pass later. Reuses
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= directly — that function
was deliberately written and tested as a reusable capability, not a
throwaway script, for exactly this.

Idempotency confirmed empirically (a manual =consolidate-history= call
right after clock-out reports "Nothing to consolidate"). *Done
differently than planned:* implemented as a full rescan of the drawer on
every clock-out rather than the incremental "only re-examine today's
entries and the newest CLOCK line" scheme originally proposed — these
drawers are a handful of lines at most, so a full regex rescan costs
nothing measurable. The incremental version remains available as a
future optimization if a heading's history ever grows large enough for
that to matter, which hasn't happened in practice.

*Relationship to "Coarsen :SESSIONS: to session-level" above:*
different mechanism, same underlying pain. That proposal changes
*when* :SESSIONS: gets written (detach from per-turn
Stop/UserPromptSubmit boundaries, write only at genuine session
close) — nothing about :LOGBOOK:'s intentionally fine-grained CLOCK
writes changes there. This proposal instead changes *what happens* to
writes that continue to happen at the current frequency, compacting
them immediately after each clock-out. If "Coarsen" ships first,
there's less to consolidate per day in the first place, but
:LOGBOOK: rounding/merging here would still apply regardless — the
two are complementary, not competing, and could both land.

*Risk:* rounding only ever touches already-closed history, so it can
never corrupt a running clock — the retrospective run and its tests
already exercise that exact boundary. The main remaining risk is
purely mechanical: forgetting to scope the on-the-fly call to *only*
the heading that was just clocked out of.

One-time retrospective cleanup of the per-turn SESSIONS/LOGBOOK churn
accumulated so far: collapse each heading's :SESSIONS: entries to one
min-to-max span per calendar day; round each :LOGBOOK: CLOCK interval
to the nearest 5-minute multiple; then, after sorting, merge any
intervals that are adjacent or overlapping post-rounding into a single
min-to-max span. Implemented as a reusable elisp function rather than a
throwaway script, so the same logic can back the on-the-fly version
scoped separately — see "Auto-consolidate SESSIONS/LOGBOOK on the fly"
under Clock lifecycle & visibility.


* DONE PreToolUse transition guard :code:

A hook on org_set_todo that inspects the requested state against
current clock state and rejects the call outright — e.g. DOING→DONE
with no open clock — before it ever reaches Emacs. Belt-and-suspenders
alongside the org-hook enforcement above; catches it one layer
earlier, specifically on the MCP path.

*Risk, resolved as planned:* fail-closed behavior when =emacsclient=
can't reach a live Emacs was implemented exactly as intended, decided
up front rather than discovered as a gap later.

*Live-verified after merge* (2026-07-29, against the real Emacs/MCP
connection, not just the mocked-emacsclient test harness): clocking
into a heading and attempting =org_set_todo= to =WAIT= on it was
correctly denied before ever reaching Emacs (heading's on-disk state
and the clock were both unchanged), and the same call succeeded
immediately after clocking out. Also incidentally established that
=.claude/settings.json= hook changes (=PreToolUse=/=PostToolUse=
specifically) take effect live within the *current* session in this
environment — contrary to a general claim in =org-dev.skill= that
hook config is only read at session start; that claim needs revisiting
(=SessionStart= hooks themselves are the one category that genuinely
can't be re-triggered mid-session, since starting a session is the
event).

PostToolUse on org_clock_in/org_clock_out → a native notification.
Small, but directly closes the trust gap this whole session kept
running into: two of the four tools silently "succeeded" while
failing to persist to disk. A confirmation outside the model's own
self-report is a cheap extra check, specifically at the moment
clock state changes.

*Risk, materialized in a different form than expected:* the tool-name
matcher itself was fine, but the =tool_response= field's real shape
for an MCP tool call turned out to be a bare JSON array of
=\{type,text\}= objects — none of the three shapes the branch's own
tests guessed at (=.text=, =.content[0].text=, bare string) matched,
so the notification body initially showed raw stringified JSON
instead of the tool's clean return string. Caught via a live
round-trip after merge (2026-07-29), fixed by adding the real shape as
the first-checked case, and locked in with a new regression test —
see the commit "Fix bin/clock-notify's tool_response extraction for
the real payload shape". The mismatch-detection logic itself was
correct throughout; only the display text was affected.

*Scope note, worth stating explicitly:* =org-clocking-p= reads
in-memory clock state only. It does *not* detect the specific
"silently failed to persist" bug class that originally motivated this
feature (a tool changing in-memory state but skipping =save-buffer=)
— if a save is silently skipped, memory and the tool's claim still
agree, and this hook reports no mismatch even though the file on disk
is stale. (See the "Tool-call audit log" entry below, and CLAUDE.md's
stale-interval-recovery section, for why disk-level checks
deliberately scan file *text* instead.)

* DONE org-trigger-hook / org-blocker-hook :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       e47ac400-dd17-4e6a-91aa-bcd837151610
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-07-29 Wed 02:06

:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
Structural enforcement inside org itself. Block DONE while a clock
is still running on that heading, or auto-open a clock the moment
DOING is set through *any* path, not just our org_set_todo wrapper —
this is the layer that also catches hand-edits made directly in
Emacs.

*Confirmed not a repeat of the org-clock-load trap:*
=org-blocker-hook=/=org-trigger-hook= are plain defvars in org.el
itself, present the instant =(provide 'org)= fires, and =add-hook=
only stores a symbol, never invokes it.

*Known limitations, deliberately accepted rather than silently
left out:*
- The blocker only matches a literal transition to =DONE=, not
  =CANCELLED= — CLAUDE.md's transition table also requires closing
  the clock before DOING→CANCELLED, but the plan's own wording said
  "denying DONE" specifically. Flagged, not expanded, since widening
  scope wasn't asked for.
- In the live Doom config (=org-clock-out-when-done t=), a direct
  DOING→DONE on a clocked heading now *blocks* instead of
  auto-closing the clock and succeeding, since the blocker hook runs
  before =org-clock-out-if-current=. This matches the plan's literal
  intent and CLAUDE.md's table, but is a real behavior change from
  the previously-frictionless auto-close — accepted as correct rather
  than reverted.

6 ERT tests added (blocker blocks/permits/scoped-to-own-heading,
trigger fires on bare =org-todo= bypassing the wrapper, trigger skips
a redundant clock-in, trigger doesn't fire for non-DOING states).
=bin/test= 39/39 at merge.

Found 2026-08-15 while checking whether a producer table in
[[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] had gone stale. It
had, and the residue is the interesting half.

=claude-code-ide-org--review-format-annotation= used to write *active*
=<>= timestamps for any item without an =:agent:=, which is every event
of an outer session. Active timestamps inside =:LOGBOOK:= reach
=org-agenda=; inactive ones do not. The branch was removed 2026-08-14 and
apply now writes inactive unconditionally — but nothing cleaned up what
the old behaviour had already written.

*Measured 2026-08-15:* TODO.org holds *55* active =<>= annotations dated
2026-08-10 through 2026-08-14, against 14 inactive ones written since the
fix. All 55 are apply-derived agent spans, or predecessors of them, and
all 55 currently render in the agenda as though they were the user's own
attention.

*Why this is a precondition and not tidying.* The active namespace inside
=:LOGBOOK:= is exactly what
[[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] intends to fill with
hand-logged attention intervals — the convention being that the agenda is
derived by reading active *timestamps* and ignoring inactive ones.
Starting that work against an agenda already ~100% populated by
mislabelled agent activity means the first attention pass cannot tell its
own entries from the noise, and every subsequent one inherits the
ambiguity.

*Open questions for whoever takes it:*

- *Delete, or convert to inactive?* Converting preserves the span and its
  label while removing it from the agenda, which is the more conservative
  move and matches what apply would write today. Deleting discards
  information that is otherwise correct — these spans really happened,
  they were merely published to the wrong audience.
- *Do the ~10 dated 2026-08-10 differ?* They predate the 2026-08-11
  cutover and so came from the older mechanism rather than from apply.
  Worth confirming they are the same shape before treating them the same
  way.
- *Is TODO.org the whole scope?* DONE.org holds archived subtrees with
  their own drawers and was not counted here.

*Do not hand-edit these without checking for a running clock or an
unsaved buffer* — 55 edits across one file is exactly the shape that
desyncs =org-clock-marker=, and the same caution the module already
applies to =org_amend= applies more here.

*Triage done 2026-08-15, and it collapses one of the open questions
above: none of the 55 are the user's own.* Asked directly whether any
active-timestamp annotation was human-authored rather than mistakenly
accepted agent output, the answer is none, on two independent grounds.

*Structural, and decisive on its own:* the active annotation is this
module's apply-time invention rather than org's — clocking manually in
Emacs writes a =CLOCK:= line and nothing else
([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]). For one of these
to be the user's, it would have had to be hand-typed in apply's exact
format. There has never been a mechanism, which is why 4f8500e6 exists.

*Signature, which agrees:* all 55 match =- <a>--<b> label= exactly. 37
carry labels of the =org_clock_in= note shape — "record post-cutover
vocabulary", "implement projected-state stale check", "drained definition
and archive command". 18 are unlabelled, and
=--review-annotation-label='s docstring records "17 such lines in one
apply" as a known output of the era before labels were synthesised. Seven
are *zero-width*, start equal to end, which apply produces from a single
guidepost and which no human would write for their own attention.

*The ~10 dated 2026-08-10 are not an exception,* which was the open
question. They predate the 2026-08-11 cutover and so came from the older
path, when =org_clock_in= wrote to the buffer directly rather than
queueing. Still agent-authored, different era. Example, from
=02aaae22='s drawer: =- <2026-08-10 Mon 19:10>--<2026-08-10 Mon 19:10>
record post-cutover vocabulary= — zero-width, agent-worded, pre-cutover.

*Consequence for this heading: no triage is needed.* The cleanup can
treat all 55 uniformly rather than sorting human from agent, which was
the expensive part. That also strengthens the "convert to inactive rather
than delete" option, since converting is lossless and there is no subset
that deserves to stay visible.

*Done 2026-08-15. Converted, not deleted — all 55, in one pass.*
Performed through Emacs rather than on disk, with the buffer reverted
first and refusing outright if it had unsaved changes, since a 55-line
rewrite under a stale buffer is the =org-clock-marker= desync this
heading warned about. No clock was running.

*The pattern was anchored at =^- <= deliberately, and the check for what
that would miss is what made it safe.* TODO.org held 124 active
timestamps but only 110 on annotation lines. The other 14 all had to
survive: a =SCHEDULED: <2026-09-13 Sun +1m>= repeater; an agenda example
in a literal block; and *quoted annotation examples inside heading
bodies*, written as =: - <...>--<...>= or inline in =…= markup, which
document what apply produced. Converting those would have falsified the
quotations rather than fixed anything. Each starts with =:= or sits
mid-line, so the anchor skips them.

*Verified by differential rather than by counting.* 110 lines changed —
55 pairs — and *every* changed line matches an annotation shape, so
nothing else moved. Line count identical at 11417. Survivors confirmed
present afterwards: the repeater, all 7 quoted examples, and all 102
=CLOCK:= lines. Active annotations now 0; inactive 69, being the 14
written since the fix plus these 55.

*Consequence: the agenda is now empty of agent activity,* and the active
=<>= namespace inside =:LOGBOOK:= is clear for
[[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] to fill with
hand-logged attention intervals. The first attention pass will be reading
an empty agenda rather than one full of mislabelled agent spans, which
was the point.


* DONE The agenda is for human attention; the queue records agent activity :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Queue
:ID:       b8e6007a-ae13-4b13-bb2e-a5df39435af0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 16:46]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-08-17 Mon 17:13
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Observability
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:

*Decided 2026-08-14, from the user's correction, and it is a design
change rather than a bug fix.* Inactive, non-=CLOCK:= timestamps are for
*any* non-human agent — including the outer REPL, not only subagents.

*The defect that surfaced it.*
=claude-code-ide-org--review-format-annotation= branched on the item's
=:agent=: inactive when present, active otherwise. Its docstring claimed
this distinguished a *human* span from a subagent's, but the test it
actually performed was "is this a subagent," and the two come apart on
the outer session. Measured: all 68 events of one session carried
=agent_id= nil, so every span it produced was published to =org-agenda=
as though the user had been at the keyboard for it. Line 5032 of this
file was one — an *active* 53-minute annotation for work Claude did.

*The fix removes the branch rather than improving it.* The queue records
agent activity and nothing else: hooks and MCP tools write it, and a
human clocking by hand in Emacs writes a bare =CLOCK:= line with no
annotation at all ([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]
established that). So no queue-derived span is ever the user's own
attention, and there is no case left to branch on. All review-applied
annotations are now inactive.

*What this narrows, deliberately.*
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]] confirmed live that
an active timestamp inside =:LOGBOOK:= reaches the agenda, and
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s guideposts were
built to exploit it — "org's own agenda doubles as a free retrospective
what-did-I-attend-to view." That mechanism still works and is still the
right one; what changes is *who may use it*. It is now reserved for
intervals a human logs themselves. The agenda answers "where did *my*
attention go"; the queue answers "what was the agent doing." Conflating
them makes the first unreadable, which is the whole complaint.

*The two-layer model this settles, in the user's own framing:* the agent
record is mechanically capturable and should therefore be *precise* —
minute-by-minute, correctly allocated, because that is the cheap half.
The human's own attention is coarse, subjective, and logged by hand,
possibly as multi-hour blocks at container level, possibly mixed with
finer entries. The precise record is *input* to that judgement, not a
substitute for it. An earlier reading of this — "go coarse everywhere,
the misattribution stops mattering" — was rejected: it would discard the
cheap accurate thing in order to approximate the expensive inexact one.

*Worked example, same day: a declared absence needs no queue event.* The
user declared AFK ~15:45–16:15 and asked what followed from it. Checked
rather than designed for: the last event before it was a =pause= at
15:42:13 and the next a =resume= at 16:44:23 — a 62-minute gap against a
threshold of 1200s, so
=claude-code-ide-org--aggregate-guideposts= already splits there and no
span straddles the absence. Nor is the queue's inability to tell "away"
from "present but not prompting" a defect under this model: the
agent-layer record correctly says *no agent activity* across that
window, which is true wherever the human was. Absence is a fact about
the human layer, and the human layer is logged by hand. Nothing to
build, which is the answer only the two-layer split makes available —
before it, this looked like it wanted a new event kind.

*Related but genuinely unfixed:*
[[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]] is the case a gap
threshold cannot reach — a turn stalled on a permission prompt emits no
=pause=, so the *agent* layer claims activity that did not happen. That
is a defect in the precise layer and this decision does not touch it.


* DONE Report abandoned DOING leaves at session start, not only WAIT :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-14 Fri 13:31]
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Meta
:ID:       9d7531f5-11c5-4203-89e3-56c3fe399df5
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 13:28]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-08-17 Mon 17:13
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Observability
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-14 Fri 13:29] \\
  implementing the report keyword change
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-14 Fri 13:31] \\
  shipped, mutation-tested, verified live
:END:

*Split out of [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] on
2026-08-14,* where it had been recorded as prose rather than as a task
and would have been archived along with that heading. The precondition
it was waiting on — a container test living somewhere reusable —
shipped with =ab75d6d2= as
=claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p=.

*The change:* =claude-code-ide-org--wait-headings-context= tests
=(equal (org-get-todo-state) "WAIT")= literally. Add =DOING= to that
test, skipping any heading the container predicate claims, and skipping
whichever heading is currently clocked, since
=--clocked-heading-context= already reports that one on its own line.

*Why the container filter is the whole point, not a refinement.*
=ab75d6d2='s finding was that the two are the same keyword and opposite
news: a container in =DOING= is a true and unremarkable statement about
the project, while a leaf in =DOING= with no clock is an increment
somebody walked away from. Without the filter this report gains one
permanent line for [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]
that never changes — and a report that always says the same thing stops
being read, which is the failure
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] already recorded
for evidence lines.

*Measured before building, 2026-08-14 13:28.* Today the change emits
*nothing*: no =WAIT= headings remain, nothing is clocked, and the only
=DOING= heading in any tracked file is =b5f7c5c7=, a container. It earns
its keep the next time a leaf is abandoned — which happened twice on
2026-08-13, when =5ff5a4b8= and
[[id:4f9e552a-e1a9-48dd-8801-3f23a31b9e32][4f9e552a]] both sat =DOING=
overnight and neither was ever mentioned at session start.

*Known ceiling, not a reason to skip it:*
[[id:c5b02503-1700-47fd-bf89-bd3296b24af2][c5b02503]] records that this
report is only as reliable as the model choosing to relay it. This makes
the report *say* the right thing; it cannot make a session pass it on.

*Outcome, 2026-08-14.* Shipped as described. =WAIT= and =DOING= are now
two branches of a =cond= rather than one literal =equal=, with the
=DOING= branch guarded twice: containers are skipped via
=claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p=, and so is whichever heading
is currently clocked, since =--clocked-heading-context= already reports
that one. Abandoned leaves render as
=DOING, not clocked: "title" (:ID: …, in file)=.

*Renamed, deliberately:* =--wait-headings-context= ->
=--attention-headings-context=. A function called "wait headings" that
reports =DOING= is precisely the silent drift this project keeps
re-finding, and the rename cost one caller. Retiring the old name took
=fmakunbound= as well as a reload — a =load-file= alone leaves the old
definition live and would have hidden a missed caller — per the org-dev
skill's own procedure.

*Verified.* Three new tests (251 total, all green), each confirmed by
mutation rather than by passing once: disabling the =DOING= branch
failed only the abandoned-leaf test, dropping the container guard failed
only the container test, and dropping the clocked-heading guard failed
only the no-duplicate test. Each mutation failed *exactly* the test
aimed at it and nothing else, which is what makes them independent
rather than three views of one assertion.

*Then verified live, and this is the part worth recording.* The report
returns the empty string against the real files — but an empty result is
also what a broken scan returns, so it was not taken as evidence on its
own. Probed alongside it: the scan sees exactly *one* =DOING= heading
across all tracked files, and that heading is
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]], for which the
container predicate answers =t=. So the silence is the filter working,
not the scan finding nothing.

*Still unproven, by construction:* whether this actually helps. Its
value shows up only the next time a leaf is abandoned overnight, and
=c5b02503='s ceiling still applies — a session has to relay it. The
honest check is to look at the first session-start report that carries a
=DOING, not clocked= line and ask whether it changed anything.

only by manual, ad-hoc disk inspection, more than once. A log
(timestamp, tool, args, file hash or diff before/after) would have
caught both immediately and gives a real trail for anything an
agent mutates unattended.

*Recommendation, followed:* implemented at the elisp level as
planned, not a Claude Code PostToolUse hook.

*Real bug caught live, exactly as this feature was designed to catch
one* (2026-07-29, immediately after this task's own merge, before its
heading was even marked DONE): clocking into a scratch heading in a
different file auto-closed this heading's own running clock, and the
audit log recorded the mutation as =UNSAVED-MISMATCH= — identical
before/after hashes despite a real edit, because
=claude-code-ide-org-clock-in='s =(save-buffer)= only ever saved the
buffer of the heading being clocked *into*, never a *different*
file's buffer that =org-clock-in= implicitly auto-closed and mutated.
This is the third instance of the exact bug class CLAUDE.md already
documents for =org_archive=/=org_clock_out= — this time in
=org_clock_in=. Fixed separately (see commit "Fix org_clock_in to
save the auto-closed previous clock's buffer"), with its own
regression test (confirmed to fail without the fix, pass with it) and
a live round-trip re-verification. The audit log's design goal —
"would have caught both immediately" — held up against a real,
previously-unknown instance of the same bug, not just the two it was
originally written to explain.

5 ERT tests added, including one using =sleep-for= (confirmed to pump
Emacs's timer queue in =--batch= mode) to prove the deferred
after-hash =run-at-time= timer fires on its own, not just via the
directly-callable =claude-code-ide-org--audit-flush= every other test
uses. =bin/test= 76/76 at merge (77/77 after the =org_clock_in= fix's
own additional test).


* DONE SessionStart context hook :code:
:PROPERTIES:

:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:

Surface "what was I last doing." On any Claude Code session start
in a project with org files, query the currently-clocked heading
(if any) and any :WAIT: items and drop them into context
automatically. Turns "what was I working on" from a question into
something Claude already knows walking in — effectively a standing,
automatic instance of whatever org_query above ends up answering on
demand.

Buffers opened via =find-file-noselect= during the query are killed again
if they weren't already open, so the user's window config isn't
disturbed. *Real bug caught in review before merge:* a bare
=(kill-buffer buffer)= on a buffer Emacs considers modified triggers an
interactive =yes-or-no-p= prompt, which blocks forever under
=emacsclient -e= (no stdin) — fixed by clearing =set-buffer-modified-p
nil= first, mirroring the shared test fixture's own teardown pattern.

8 ERT tests added for the elisp half (empty/clocked-only/WAIT-only/
both/non-WAIT-ignored/buffer-kill-hygiene cases); the shell script's
=emacsclient --eval= round trip against a live buffer-list is the one
piece that could only be exercised by the coordinator post-merge, not
from the isolated worktree. =bin/test= 68/68 at merge.

Captured 2026-08-05 from conversation, surfaced while reviewing the
PLANNING-state feature: =claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string='s
=status-label= only ever shows "clocked in" or "clocked out"
(=org-clocking-p= test), which conflates states this project now
distinguishes — a =PLANNING= task and a =DOING= task both read "clocked
in" even though they mean different things (still designing vs.
actively implementing), and a =WAIT= task and a =DONE= task pulled from
=org-clock-history= both read "clocked out" even though one is blocked
and the other finished.

Fix: swap =status-label= for the heading's actual TODO keyword
(=org-get-todo-state= at the clock marker's position, already inside
the =org-with-point-at marker= block =status-label= is computed in)
instead of the binary =org-clocking-p= check. Needs a =bin/test= update
for each of the five existing =--statusline-task-string= regression
tests, which likely assert on the literal "clocked in"/"clocked out"
string.

*Note added 2026-08-05:* worth reading against
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]
before implementing — =org-get-todo-state= at the clock marker is
still marker-dependent, so this fix inherits that same desync mode
(would show a stale TODO keyword instead of a stale in/out label,
rather than routing around the underlying fragility). Doesn't block
this task; just don't be surprised if the new failure mode looks
similar to the old one.

*Cancelled 2026-08-14, folded into
[[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]].* Not declined on
merit — the complaint is real and still stands, and it now lives there
as a requirement. Cancelled because the *mechanism* proposed above was
superseded by the cutover, which happened after this was written.

*Why reading the keyword at the clock marker no longer works.* Two
compounding reasons, both consequences of the queue architecture:

1. =org-clocking-p= is essentially never true. A clock is live only for
   the microseconds between the paired =org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out=
   inside apply — [[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]]
   records exactly this in its vocabulary notes. So
   =--statusline-task-string= almost always falls through to
   =(car org-clock-history)=, which is why the statusline sits on the
   last *applied* heading rather than the one being worked.
2. The keyword at that marker is the *on-disk* one, which lags the queue
   by a whole review pass. Measured 2026-08-14: while this note was
   being written, [[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]
   read =DOING= on disk with =DONE= queued and unapplied.

So the fix as specified would replace a stale binary label with a stale
keyword — the same failure mode in richer vocabulary. The note above
already saw the shape of this ("inherits that same desync mode... rather
than routing around the underlying fragility"); it simply could not name
=290b6fc5= as the routing-around, because that heading did not exist
yet.

*Also worth recording, since it is three signals in one direction:* this
heading was auto-promoted to =NEXT= on 2026-08-06, hand-demoted to
=TODO= the same day with no implementation attempted (noted on
[[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][c954f650]] as evidence
against =PLANNING='s weight), and later dropped to =MAYBE=. It has been
declined every time it came up.

*Kept, not lost:* the substance — a statusline label should distinguish
=PLANNING= from =DOING= and =WAIT= from =DONE=, rather than collapsing
all four into clocked in/out — is now the third bullet of =290b6fc5='s
instruction list. The five =--statusline-task-string= regression tests
this would have rewritten get rewritten once, there, instead of twice.

Proposed 2026-08-13 after a day of running the checks by hand. The
=id:=-link resolver below was run manually before nearly every commit
that day — roughly twenty times — which is the plainest possible signal
that it wants to be a script.

*Assertions, all decidable without judgement:*
- Every =[[id:...]]= link resolves to a heading that exists in
  =TODO.org= or =DONE.org=. Caught four *fabricated* UUIDs in one
  session; each had the right 8-character prefix and 28 invented hex
  digits, which reads as correct and fails only when followed.
- No level-4 headings. The file has exactly three levels — category,
  task, epic-child — across 624 headings, and nothing enforces it.
- Every heading below level 1 carries =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=; every
  level-1 heading carries neither, plus no TODO keyword and no tags.
  That is CLAUDE.md's stated convention and it is entirely checkable.
- *A heading with a repeater has no completable ancestor.* A =+1m= task
  never reaches =DONE=, so an epic containing one never can — which had
  silently frozen [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]]
  via its =:BLOCKER:=. This is the check that would have caught a real
  problem nobody was looking for.
- Every =:BLOCKER:= names an =:ID:= that exists *and carries a TODO
  keyword*. The second half is not pedantry: =org-depend= blocks only on
  an *unfinished TODO*, so a blocker naming a keyword-less heading is a
  silent no-op. Found 2026-08-13 within minutes of adding one — the
  target was a heading =org_capture= had created, whose =NEXT= was still
  queued and unapplied, so it was keyword-less on disk. That window is a
  *consequence of the queue architecture*: every captured heading is
  keyword-less until a human applies its state, so any blocker naming a
  fresh capture is inert for exactly as long as the review pass is
  deferred. Checking only that the id resolves would have passed.

  *And a third clause, found the same hour:* blocking is evaluated
  against the *blocked heading's own state*. A =:BLOCKER:= on a =MAYBE=
  heading reports not-blocked even when its target is unfinished, so it
  looks enforcing and is not. Both the second and third clauses were
  discovered by adding a blocker and checking whether it fired — which
  is the shape this lint should encode: not "is the property
  well-formed" but "would it actually block".
- Every =[[file:~/.claude/plans/...]]= link points at a file that
  exists. =bin/sync-plans --check= covers the archive side of this but
  not dangling links.

*Reuse the harness rather than building a third one.*
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] wants
=bin/check-claude-md= for CLAUDE.md's checkable claims, and
[[id:d7849119-8224-4993-a8ab-83e1434df53c][d7849119]] wants README
consistency guarded the same way; =02aaae22= already records that
whichever lands first should build the harness so the others only add
assertions. This is the third assertion set, not a third program. The
shape is settled: =bin/sync-plans --check= plus the =.githooks/pre-push=
guard, which refuses a push while a document and what it describes
disagree.

*Why this is its own task rather than scope on either of those.* Those
two check claims *about* the system — does a named function exist, does
a documented line match the file. This checks the org files' *own*
structural conventions. Neither would be incomplete without it, which is
the test that separated three headings out of
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] the same day.

*One caution for whoever builds it.* Two of these assertions describe
conventions that are currently *unbroken*, so the lint will pass on day
one and prove nothing. Seed it against a deliberately broken copy first
— the same discipline as confirming a regression test fails without its
fix, which caught two vacuous tests that day.

*And an honest limit on the practice, measured rather than assumed.*
Automating consistency enforcement is the right instinct — this repo
already runs the pattern four times over (=bin/sync-plans --check= plus
the pre-push guard, =bin/check-claude-md= proposed on
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]],
[[id:d7849119-8224-4993-a8ab-83e1434df53c][d7849119]]'s README check,
and this). But the mechanically checkable subset is *narrower than the
drift actually observed*. Four stale statements surfaced on 2026-08-13:

| stale statement | checkable? |
|-----------------+------------|
| CLAUDE.md's =#+TODO:= line omits the =(t!)= markers the files carry | *yes* — string compare against line 1 |
| =--aggregate-guideposts= says consolidation rounds; it stopped in =b74e0f19= | *no* — prose about behaviour |
| =consolidate-history='s docstring says "newest-first"; =af7d3687= made it ascending | *barely* — would need to compare a docstring's claim to a sort order |
| the epic's "no writer but apply touches these files" | *no* — a claim about five tools, a skill and git |

So a lint catches the *shape* of drift — a missing =:ID:=, a dangling
link, a repeater under a completable parent — and misses the *reasoning*
kind, which is where today's genuinely dangerous staleness lived. Both
kinds read as settled fact; only one is a string. Worth stating so the
harness is not mistaken for coverage it does not have.

*Outcome, 2026-08-14, and it opens with a correction that matters more
than the script does.* Asked whether org's built-in =org-lint= had been
considered: it had not. That is precisely the failure
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]] exists to prevent
— "do not treat this project's own prior choices as proof no better
native mechanism exists" — committed *hours after closing that very
heading*. The instruction was followed for the queue architecture and
skipped for the next thing built.

*What running it showed, measured rather than argued.* =org-lint= ships
61 checkers, and on these files it reports *11 "Possibly misplaced
heading line"* findings across =TODO.org= and =DONE.org= that the
hand-written checks miss entirely. That is the same class as the defect
found here by accident — a heading glued to the end of a prose line,
introduced in =f39944f= on 2026-08-13, which org therefore never saw as
a heading at all, leaving its =:ID:= unreachable and two links to it
dangling. So org already had a checker for the thing this task
discovered by hand.

*The division of labour, now that both are running.* =bin/lint-org=
invokes =org-lint= and folds its findings in as warnings. The two ask
different questions and neither subsumes the other: =org-lint= asks
whether this is valid *org*, and the checks written here ask whether it
obeys *this project's* conventions — three heading levels, =:ID:= and
=:CREATED:= on every task, bare categories, a =:BLOCKER:= that would
actually block, no repeater under a completable ancestor. No built-in
could know those. But the id-link and file-link checks *did* overlap,
and would not have been written at all had org-lint been run first.

*One exclusion, an artifact rather than a disagreement.* org-lint's
Unknown-ID checker resolves =id:= links through =org-id-locations=,
which a live session populates and =emacs --batch -Q= does not —
unfiltered it reported all 373 =id:= links in =TODO.org= as unknown. The
check that runs instead resolves ids from the linted files themselves,
so it works in batch, which is where a gate has to work.

*Severity, added because a gate has to be usable.* Errors fail the run;
warnings report. Not cosmetic: 13 headings archived in =DONE.org=
predate the =:CREATED:= rule, and back-dating them would fabricate a
fact rather than fix one. A report carrying the same 13 lines forever
stops being read, which is
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]]'s finding arriving
by another route.

*What it found on first run, against the caution above that it would
"pass on day one and prove nothing":* 5 headings with no =:ID:= at all
(now fixed, with =:CREATED:= recovered from git history rather than
stamped with today's date), the glued heading described above, 3 dormant
=:BLOCKER:=s on =MAYBE= headings, and the 11 org-lint reports. The
caution was wrong, and pleasantly so.

*Verified.* 11 tests, 266 green, each check fed a fixture that violates
it — the heading's "seed it against a deliberately broken copy first"
discharged mechanically rather than remembered. Includes a positive
control asserting a clean file reports nothing, without which the others
could pass by the lint flagging everything. One fixture bug worth
recording: the first version omitted the =#+TODO:= header, so org did
not recognise =MAYBE= as a keyword and the dormant-blocker assertion
silently could not fire.

*Left open:* the 11 misplaced-heading reports are surfaced, not
diagnosed — filed as
[[id:8d682a5b-1b03-4744-995c-a871a8b40ddc][8d682a5b]].


* DONE emacs is a shell function that silently breaks inline batch verification :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-14 Fri 12:17]
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Dev

:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 15:52]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-08-17 Mon 17:13
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Tooling
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-14 Fri 08:57] \\
  adding the skill line it proposes
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 09:09]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:10] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-14 Fri 09:09]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:10] task definition
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 09:22]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:25] =>  0:03
- [2026-08-14 Fri 09:22]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:25] task definition
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 09:30]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:33] =>  0:03
- [2026-08-14 Fri 09:30]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:33] task definition
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 09:37]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:42] =>  0:05
- [2026-08-14 Fri 09:37]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:42] task definition
- State "TODO"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-14 Fri 12:04]
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-14 Fri 12:17] \\
  retcon: shipped 08-14, outcome note added
:END:

The user's shell profile defines:

: emacs () { if [[ "$*" == *-nw* ]]; then command emacs "$@"; else open -a Emacs --args "$@"; fi }

Any =emacs --batch ...= run from an interactive-profile shell therefore
launches the GUI app and returns immediately: exit 0, no stdout, no
stderr. Indistinguishable from a clean run that found nothing. Hit three
times in a row on 2026-08-11 before being noticed, and nearly recorded
as a genuine "org logs nothing" finding.

=bin/test= is unaffected — a =#!/usr/bin/env bash= script gets a
non-interactive shell where the function is not defined, so =exec emacs=
resolves to =/opt/homebrew/bin/emacs=. The trap is specific to inline
one-off verification, which is exactly where the CLAUDE.md rule about
stating the reload precondition is meant to apply.

Fix is a convention, not code: use =command emacs= or the absolute path
for any batch invocation. Worth a line in the org-dev skill, which
exists for exactly this class of "did the change take effect" check.

*Narrowed 2026-08-14: the environment half of this is not this project's
to own, and now lives in
[[id:52ede10e-5e6f-4392-b31d-b9ce9b450c3c][52ede10e]] in
=~/org/notes.org=.*

What stays here, because it impinges directly on this repo:

- =emacs --batch= run inline from a session in this repo does nothing and
  reports success, so any =check-parens=, byte-compile or =--eval= probe
  is vacuous. Recurred twice on 2026-08-14 — two paren checks, one after
  a large =config.el= edit and one after a hand-resolved merge conflict,
  both believed and both worthless.
- The convention: =command emacs= or an absolute path, never the bare
  name.
- The org-dev skill now carries that warning under its section 0
  (=d34c809=), which is what this heading originally proposed and what
  makes the rule fire at the moment of the mistake rather than sitting in
  a backlog.
- =bin/test= says =exec command emacs=. It was already safe — a
  =#!/usr/bin/env bash= script gets a non-interactive shell where no such
  function is defined — but a script in the repo that documents this
  hazard should not itself read as if unaware of it.

What moved out: the shell configuration itself — three copies of the
function across zsh and fish, one of them permanently shadowed and
unreachable; the migration to =emacsclient=-based =e=/=et= aliases;
=$EDITOR=; and a plaintext credential found in =config.fish= while
looking. *The two halves fix different things and neither replaces the
other:* deleting the function fixes one machine, while the convention and
the skill line protect any machine this project is ever installed on,
including one whose shell nobody here controls.

*One finding from the split is worth keeping on this side,* because it
explains why the bug was hard to see: fish is the login shell, but a
Claude Code Bash call runs *zsh* (=SHELL=/bin/zsh=, and the shell
snapshot it replays is a zsh snapshot). So the copy that breaks this
project's verification is the =~/.zshrc= one, and someone reasoning from
"my shell is fish" would look in the wrong file.

The split also prompted naming a goal that had never been written down —
applying these org conventions at the user level, across projects rather than
inside one repo — recorded as
[[id:9ae4b17e-278c-482f-9114-df28b2254806][9ae4b17e]] (originally
=ae2d9b4a= in =notes.org=, merged here 2026-08-15).

*Outcome, 2026-08-14.* Three artifacts, each confirmed present on disk
rather than recalled: the org-dev skill carries the warning as its own
section heading, "Never invoke bare =emacs= for a batch check — use
=command emacs=" (=d34c809=); =bin/test= says =exec command emacs=, with
a comment explaining that it was already safe and says so anyway
(=ca5d96c=); and the environment half is filed as
[[id:52ede10e-5e6f-4392-b31d-b9ce9b450c3c][52ede10e]] in =~/org/notes.org=,
still open there and deliberately not tracked from here.

*How it was verified, stated plainly because it is weaker than a test.*
The deliverable is a convention plus a line of documentation, so there is
no mechanical surface to assert against — the check was reading the three
artifacts, not running anything. Whether the skill line actually fires at
the moment of the mistake is trigger-matching against a skill
description, which this project's engineering-practices rule names as
inherently fuzzy and not worth forcing into a deterministic test. The
honest evidence is negative and thin: no bare =emacs --batch= has
recurred since the skill line landed at 08:58, against two recurrences in
the hours before it.

*Why this heading's =:LOGBOOK:= reads oddly, recorded so it is not
re-diagnosed later.* It was demoted =DOING= -> =TODO= by hand at 12:04
and closed to =DONE= shortly after, which looks like work that started
and stopped. It did not. The 09:09--09:42 span was re-annotated *task
definition* at review, and the commit record agrees: =d34c809= landed at
08:58, twelve minutes after the =DOING= transition and *before* that span
opened. The implementation was the twelve minutes; the span that looks
like it was the narrowing.

Surfaced 2026-08-11 by =bin/sync-plans=, which archives a plan only when
some heading in TODO.org or DONE.org links it — so org membership is the
rule, and a plan nothing links stays outside version control and gets
reported rather than silently swept in. Four are in that state, and all
four are this project's work:

- =parsed-gliding-corbato.md= — *byte-identical to* =review-and-cutover.md=,
  written twenty-one minutes earlier on 2026-08-07. An orphan left behind
  when the auto-generated slug was replaced by a meaningful name. Almost
  certainly just delete it; confirm nothing links it first.
- =cheerful-whistling-sketch.md= — "Fix org-agenda-files resolving to
  nothing (agenda + org_query/org_clock_report)".
- =send-each-mcp-feature-zesty-bunny.md= — "Parallelize the 10 pending
  MCP-tool tasks across worktrees".
- =warp-mcp-proxy-emacs.md= — the Warp proxy investigation, whose outcome
  CLAUDE.md already describes; its heading is archived in DONE.org
  (=6a6d5b4e=) but carries no Plan link.

For the three real ones the fix is a Plan link on the owning heading,
per CLAUDE.md's rule that a plan file's existence and its link are
supposed to arrive together. Their absence is evidence the rule was
added after some of this work, not that it was ignored.

*Worth doing before the next plan revision,* because the archive only
covers what is linked: an unlinked plan is the one case where a revision
still overwrites history with no way back — the exact gap the archive
was built to close.

*Outcome, 2026-08-12.* =bin/sync-plans= now reports *0 unlinked at
source*, 26 in sync. Two linked, two retired:

- =parsed-gliding-corbato.md= — *deleted.* Confirmed byte-identical to
  =review-and-cutover.md=, which is already archived in =plans/=, so no
  content was lost, and confirmed nothing linked it first. Exactly as
  this heading predicted.
- =cheerful-whistling-sketch.md= — *linked* from
  [[id:74f24402-978e-4c66-ac97-996f387c0ddb][74f24402]] in DONE.org, the
  org-agenda-files fix. The one case that went as written.
- =warp-mcp-proxy-emacs.md= — *deleted*, on the user's call.
- =send-each-mcp-feature-zesty-bunny.md= — *linked* from a new
  retrospective heading,
  [[id:3d862c6c-6d03-4fe9-9923-067966ef943a][3d862c6c]], also on the
  user's call.

*This heading was wrong about two of the four, and the errors are the
interesting part.*

1. It said 6a6d5b4e "carries no Plan link." It does — to
   =use-pipx-instead-use-starry-metcalfe.md=, which is archived.
   =warp-mcp-proxy-emacs.md= is a *second, earlier* plan for the same
   heading: the proxy approach abandoned once the real bug turned out to
   be the =200=-vs-=202= response. So the choice was never "add the
   missing link"; it was "what do you do when one heading has two
   plans," which CLAUDE.md's *single* permanent link rule does not
   cover, since it assumes revisions reuse one file. Retired rather than
   double-linked — the heading already links the plan that reflects what
   shipped, and both its own body and CLAUDE.md narrate why the proxy
   was unnecessary.
2. It assumed all three "real ones" had owning headings. The
   parallelization plan had none: its ten tasks are archived flat in
   DONE.org and the "New MCP tools" section that grouped them no longer
   exists, so archiving dissolved the only structure that could have
   owned it. Hence a new heading rather than a link.

*Correction, same day, before this claim spread any further.* The
paragraph here originally read "archiving flattens — a plan scoped to a
section loses its owner the moment that section's members are archived
out." *That is false*, and it was asserted without checking:

- =org-archive-save-context-info= includes =olpath= by default and is
  writing it. 18 of DONE.org's 22 archived headings carry
  =:ARCHIVE_OLPATH:=, and all ten MCP-tool tasks record
  =Roadmap/New MCP tools=. The outline path is preserved, not lost. The
  four without one were archived from level 1, where there was no path.
- What actually removed the section was =b716d37= (2026-08-04),
  "Reflect completed clock-subsystem work back into the roadmap" — a
  deliberate manual restructuring that deleted the =* Roadmap= wrapper
  and promoted every section a level. Archiving behaved correctly
  throughout; a hand edit a week later did the removing.

*What is actually true, and it is narrower:* a plan spanning several
headings has no linkable owner, because =sync-plans= requires a link and
a grouping heading is structural — it carries no =:ID:= by convention
and gets deleted when it empties. So the fix is a convention, not a
mechanism: *create an umbrella heading with an =:ID:= when a plan spans
several tasks*, rather than reconstructing one later from the plan's own
table. That much still belongs in
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]].

*Worth noting how the wrong version nearly survived.* The first check
run against DONE.org reported =org_capture= as having no
=:ARCHIVE_OLPATH:= while a =sed= of the same heading printed the
property two lines above — the script tested list membership of a whole
line where it needed a substring. Same shape as the drawer-scoped grep
failures already recorded above: a wrong answer that looks like a
finding.

Was living entirely outside the repo (=~/.claude/statusline-command.fish=
+ a =statusLine= entry in the user's personal =~/.claude/settings.json=),
so nothing about it was version-controlled or shared with anyone who
clones this project. Confirmed project-scoped =statusLine= is supported
(same settings-load hierarchy as =hooks=, which this repo's own
=.claude/settings.json= already proves works at project scope), so
there's no reason it has to stay a personal, undiscoverable setup step.

*Verified, not just implemented:*
1. Relocated the script into the repo as =bin/statusline.fish= (renamed
   from extension-less =bin/statusline= after noticing it's the only
   fish-dependent script in the whole project — worth being visible
   from the filename, not just the shebang; see the sibling "Evaluate
   choice of shell" entry below, spun off from noticing this).
2. Pulled the inline Elisp (previously a large string embedded in the
   shell script's =emacsclient -e= call) out into real, named,
   testable functions in =config.el=
   (=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=,
   =claude-code-ide-org-write-statusline-report=), matching the
   existing =claude-code-ide-org-write-session-start-report=/
   =-write-session-context-report= pattern exactly. =bin/statusline.fish=
   is now a thin wrapper, same shape as =.claude/hooks/session-context.sh=.
3. Added five regression tests in =config-test.el= covering: nothing
   clocked and no history (empty string), a running clock, a
   clocked-out task from =org-clock-history=, long-name truncation, and
   that a running clock always wins over history even if history's head
   points elsewhere. =bin/test= passes 90/90.
4. Wired a =statusLine= entry into this repo's own =.claude/settings.json=
   pointing at =bin/statusline.fish= via =${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}=, same
   pattern every hook command in that file already uses. Validated with
   =jq -e= and ran the exact configured command
   (=fish ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/bin/statusline.fish=) directly — correct
   output.
5. Removed the now-redundant =~/.claude/statusline-command.fish= and the
   =statusLine= key from the user's personal =~/.claude/settings.json=
   (validated the edited file with =jq -e .= afterward) — exactly one
   copy in play now, and it ships with the repo for anyone who clones
   it, no personal setup step required.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/sharded-tickling-snowflake.md][Plan]]

Sibling entry below ("Evaluate choice of shell...") lists five options
for standardizing this project's =bin/= scripts, reordered to lead with
"pure Emacs" (push all JSON parsing/logic into Elisp, shell wrapper
shrinks to a stub). This applies that option concretely to the
statusline feature — the =jq='based model-name extraction still living
in =bin/statusline.fish= moves into Elisp, paired with a POSIX =sh=
wrapper — as a real case study the rest of that decision can be judged
against instead of staying hypothetical.

*Verified, not just implemented:* new
=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-model-name= reads and parses the
statusLine hook JSON payload directly (=json-read-file=, no =jq=);
=claude-code-ide-org-write-statusline-report= gained an =input-path=
parameter and now writes the model name followed by the task info to
=output-path= — Elisp produces the *entire* final statusline text.
=bin/statusline.fish= replaced by =bin/statusline.sh=: plain POSIX
=sh=, six lines, no =jq=, no field/string handling of any kind — just
write stdin to a temp file, call =emacsclient=, cat the result. Six new
regression tests (well-formed payload, missing field, malformed JSON,
missing input file, and an end-to-end combined-output test); all 5
pre-existing =--statusline-task-string= tests pass unchanged. =bin/test=
passes 95/95. =.claude/settings.json='s =statusLine.command= updated to
=sh ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/bin/statusline.sh=, validated with =jq -e=.
Live: reloaded =config.el=, ran the exact configured command end-to-end
against real clock state — correct output, model name and task info
both present, no fish, no =jq= anywhere in the pipeline.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/cheerful-whistling-sketch.md][Plan]]

Doom's =org-agenda-files= is =("~/org")= — a bare directory string —
and org's directory expansion for it is non-recursive, so
=(org-agenda-files)= resolves to nil (TODO.org is symlinked one level
below =~/org=, inside =claude-code-ide-org/=). The interactive agenda
never sees it.

Worse: =claude-code-ide-org--tracked-files= (config.el:412) returns
the *raw* =org-agenda-files= variable rather than calling the
=(org-agenda-files)= function, so =org_query= and unscoped
=org_clock_report= are also blind whenever they fall back to it —
confirmed live: =(claude-code-ide-org-query "(todo)")= returned "No
matches." despite an active DOING heading. Affects three consumers:
the stale-interval recovery scan, =org_query=, and unscoped
=org_clock_report=. No existing test exercises the directory-fallback
path — every test binds =claude-code-ide-org-query-files= directly.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =bin/test= (78/78 including the new
regression test), plus live checks against the running Emacs after
reloading both the Doom config setq (=emacsclient -e '(setq
org-agenda-files ...)'=, since it doesn't touch any =after!=/hook
timing — no restart needed per org-dev.skill's rule of thumb) and
config.el itself (=emacsclient -e '(load-file
"modules/tools/claude-code-ide-org/config.el")'=): =(org-agenda-files)=
now resolves to TODO.org's real path; =org_query "todo:DOING"= finds
both DOING headings instead of "No matches."; the interactive
=org-todo-list= renders every heading in this file; =org_clock_report=
returns real clocked time instead of an empty table.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/use-pipx-instead-use-starry-metcalfe.md][Plan]]

Warp's own agent (already used on this repo — see commits
co-authored by =Oz <oz-agent@warp.dev>=) needed a bridge to reach the
Emacs MCP tools server. The initial hypothesis — that Emacs' HTTP
server closes each connection with no SSE fallback, and Warp's
persistent-connection client would trip over that — turned out to be
a red herring.

*Verified, not just implemented:* the plan's falsify-first Step A (a
direct-HTTP =.warp/.mcp.json=, no proxy) surfaced the real bug in
Warp's logs instead: =notifications/initialized= failed with
"Transport channel closed". Root cause:
=claude-code-ide-mcp-http-server--send-empty-response= replied =200=
+ =text/plain= to that notification where the MCP spec expects =202
Accepted=. Fixed via a Doom config override (pending an upstream
report — see the sibling subtask above); confirmed both via a direct
=mcp-proxy= round-trip and live in Warp itself. With the real bug
fixed, the direct-HTTP entry alone was sufficient — no proxy needed.
The =pipx=/=mcp-proxy= stdio bridge explored along the way (which hit
its own unrelated dependency breakage) was never actually required
and has been uninstalled; nothing repo-tracked depends on it. Both
=.mcp.json= and =.warp/.mcp.json= are kept regardless, even though
currently identical, as a deliberate seam for the two clients to
diverge later.

CLAUDE.md's Plan-checklist reconciliation rule (and org.skill's matching
checklist-discipline guidance) is being discarded entirely as unnecessary
overhead — a plan tracked twice (org checklist + Claude Code's own Plan
Mode file) that then needs keeping in sync. Replacing with: a permanent
link to the Plan Mode file, kept through DONE, plus a concise prose outcome
summary at completion instead of checkbox reconciliation. Also migrating
org.skill/org-dev.skill off their non-standard zip-archive packaging to
Claude Code's actual native format (.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md, plain
text) since both files are being edited anyway. See the linked plan for
full scope.

*Shipped as planned, two commits:* (1) org.skill/org-dev.skill migrated
verbatim to =.claude/skills/org/SKILL.md=/=.claude/skills/org-dev/SKILL.md=
— confirmed via Claude Code's own docs that zip archives were never a
supported skill format, and that this repo had invented the convention
itself. =bin/check-org-dev-skill='s zip-structure check replaced with a
plain-text existence check; org-dev/SKILL.md's now-obsolete unzip/edit/
rezip section removed. (2) the reconciliation rule removed from CLAUDE.md
and replaced with the plan-link + concise-summary rules above; matching
checklist-discipline guidance removed from the org skill; all 15
checklists (each a =**** Plan= sub-heading with an =[N/M]= cookie) found
across TODO.org/DONE.org stripped (one more
than originally scoped — the still-unarchived "Fix org-agenda-files"
DONE heading above also had one), folding any non-redundant verification
claim or implementation deviation into surrounding prose rather than
losing it. The one deviation from the approved plan: per the user's
mid-task instruction, the new-heading-approval-gate step was itself
promoted from a one-off sequencing note into a standing CLAUDE.md rule
(see "State transition rules") rather than living only in the plan file.
Verified: =bin/check-org-dev-skill= passes; =grep -n "Plan \["
TODO.org DONE.org= returns zero matches; =org_query "todo:DOING"= shows
only this heading while it was active.


* DONE Revisit: does org.skill's checkbox discipline apply to TODO.org itself? :research:code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Skill
:ID:       6a448345-016c-495f-b26b-ee81b30cd95c
:CREATED:  [2026-07-29 Wed 07:42]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-07-29 Wed 18:47
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Roadmap/Developer tooling
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-07-29 Wed 11:45]--[2026-07-29 Wed 12:05] =>  0:20
:END:
Tonight's parallel-subagent batch (10 MCP tools) left every archived
heading's Plan checkboxes at all-[ ], despite full implementation —
org.skill's existing "check off as you go" discipline was never
applied. Fixed retroactively (DONE.org reconciled by hand) plus a new
CLAUDE.md rule making it a coordinator responsibility. But a later
conversation pushed back on the reasoning behind that rule, and it got
meta enough to warrant its own session rather than more tail-end
discussion bolted onto an already-long one.

*Why TODO.org is a special case, not a template for org.skill in
general:* org.skill normally applies to a user's .org files living in
arbitrary other projects/folders — files with no relationship to a
git repo whose code is simultaneously being changed by parallel
subagents. TODO.org here is unusual on two axes at once: (1) it's
this repo's own tracked source file, in the same repo as the code
the subagents are editing, and (2) it's edited through two different
channels — live-Emacs/emacsclient (state, clock, archive) and
git-tracked branch diffs (plan-checkbox text) — that don't normally
have to coexist for an arbitrary end-user org file.

*Where the discussion landed, tentatively, before deferring:* the
real risk this session wasn't "two subagents' diffs collide" (rare —
different headings, same shape as config.el's clean merges) — it was
that the coordinator (this session) batched all TODO.org/DONE.org
commits at the very end instead of committing incrementally per
task, the same cadence used for config.el. That left git's tracked
TODO.org frozen at its pre-session state for the whole session while
the live-Emacs-edited on-disk file kept drifting — a mismatch that
has nothing to do with how many subagents are running or what they
touch. Tentative fix direction: commit TODO.org per-task (not
batched); let a subagent check off its own heading's boxes/decisions
directly in its own worktree; rely on the already-enabled
global-auto-revert-mode to sync the live buffer after a merge before
archiving that heading.

*Resolved:* the fresh session concluded the whole reconciliation
mechanism was the wrong fix — not "who reconciles it, coordinator or
subagent" but "why maintain a second copy of the plan in org at all."
Claude Code's own Plan Mode already persists the plan to a durable file
(=~/.claude/plans/<slug>.md=, confirmed empirically to never be cleaned
up); org now carries a permanent link to that file plus a concise prose
outcome summary at =DONE=, never a transcribed checklist. This
generalizes past this repo's own meta-bookkeeping file: the
reconciliation rule is gone from CLAUDE.md entirely, and the matching
checklist-discipline guidance is gone from org.skill too, so "does this
apply to org.skill in general" is moot — there's no checklist
convention left for it to apply to. The auto-revert/explicit-revert-buffer
question is moot for the same reason: with no plan text transcribed into
org, there's nothing left to keep in sync between git and the live
buffer, so no revert-timing race this rule needs to guard against. See
TODO.org's "Discard the Plan-checklist convention" heading and its
linked plan for the full resolution.

A development-tooling skill packaging the reload-and-verify
procedure this session had to rediscover by hand, more than once:
the =after!=/hook-ordering bug that only surfaced after a real
Emacs restart, =bin/test= silently running against the wrong
bundled org version for a while, and =org.skill='s zip needing
unzip/edit/rezip rather than a direct text edit. Different from
=org.skill= — that one is for editing =.org= file *content* for end
users; this one is for reloading and verifying changes to *this
project's own code* (config.el, hooks, bin/test).

*Scope limit, decided already:* the Emacs-reload half can be fully
automated (deterministic, safe to just do). The Claude-Code-hook half
can only be *flagged*, never performed — nothing running inside a
session can force that same session to restart. The honest shape is
"auto-reload Emacs, then tell the user a new session is needed if
=.claude/settings.json= changed," not a single button that does
everything.

*Follow-up:* [2/2] — per the new "test new features" rule in CLAUDE.md
- [X] Content-accuracy check: =bin/check-org-dev-skill= (fish), a
  doctest for the skill in the same spirit as =bin/test= catching
  drift in the wrapper functions. Asserts, against live state rather
  than parsing the fences generically: =emacsclient -e
  '(claude-code-ide-mcp-server-get-port)'= returns =45571= and matches
  =.mcp.json=; =org.skill=/=org-dev.skill= each contain exactly one
  zip entry at the documented internal path; =bin/test='s =build-*=
  glob resolves to a directory containing =org/=; and the
  =config.el:410=/=config.el:457= line references cited in
  =org-dev/SKILL.md= still point at the tool-registration block and
  file end. All checks pass as of [2026-07-28 Tue].
- [X] Explicitly out of scope for automation: whether the skill
  actually *triggers* on its description's trigger phrases — that's
  semantic matching decided fresh each session, not deterministic;
  verify by spot-checking fresh sessions against representative
  prompts instead, don't force it into a hard test. (Decision stands
  as-is — no code follow-up needed, recorded here per CLAUDE.md's rule
  to say so explicitly rather than skip silently.)


* DONE Make check-org-dev-skill line-number checks robust to config.el drift :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Dev
:ID:       9be21b6b-5219-4acd-950a-e04b60d8fe1e
:CREATED:  [2026-07-28 Tue 13:56]
:ARCHIVE_TIME: 2026-07-29 Wed 08:11
:ARCHIVE_FILE: ~/git/claude-code-ide-org/TODO.org
:ARCHIVE_OLPATH: Roadmap/Developer tooling
:ARCHIVE_CATEGORY: TODO
:ARCHIVE_TODO: DONE
:END:
=bin/check-org-dev-skill='s tool-registration-block-start and
last-line checks hardcode absolute =config.el= line numbers (410/457,
then 462/528, now 688/754 after the =consolidate_sessions_logbook=
merge). Every =config.el= edit that changes line count breaks these
two checks again immediately — confirmed twice in one day. Redesign
so the check doesn't hardcode absolute line numbers (e.g. anchor on a
nearby stable string/regex, or compute an expected relationship rather
than a magic number) instead of just re-editing the constants each
time drift is noticed.

*Done, on =feature/robust-check-org-dev-skill-lines=:* the two checks
now extract the expected =NNN-MMM= range straight out of the
=org-dev/SKILL.md= citation text (via =unzip -p= + a regex) instead of
carrying a second hardcoded copy in the script. Single source of truth
is the doc, which already needs a human edit whenever the cited range
moves — this just stops the script from needing its own, independent
edit for the same drift.

*Caveat, precisely:* this is consolidation, not immunity. A
=config.el= edit that shifts these lines will still make the check
FAIL — verified by temporarily inserting blank lines into =config.el=
and confirming both checks fail with a nonzero exit, then restoring
and confirming they pass again. The one remaining edit needed
afterward is to =org-dev/SKILL.md='s citation (already required for
human readers), not also to =bin/check-org-dev-skill=. Dropping the
exact line numbers from the doc entirely (e.g. citing the
=with-eval-after-load= anchor with no numbers) would remove drift
pressure altogether, but that's a doc-content trade-off — it makes the
doc's own reference to readers less precise — left for a separate
decision rather than made unilaterally here.
